1. Thank you for your letter of 26 August addressed to the Prime

Minister regarding “unidentified flying objects". Your letter has

been passed to the Ministry of Defence and this office is the

focal point within the MOD for correspondence of this nature. I
-- would like to apologise for the delay in replying.

2. It remains the case that the Ministry of Defence examines any.
-reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
‘significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised’ by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity. Unless there is evidence
of a potential military threat we do not attempt to. identify the
precise nature of each report. This was set out to you in my
letter of 14 March, and the government's stance with respect to
reports of "unexplained" aerial sightings has not altered since

then.
Nous sin Quy,

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

The attached letter(s) which the-Prime-Minister has received has been
forwarded to this Department for official action. No.10's letter codes are as
follows:

A - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
send a full reply within 20 working days.

B - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
consider whether there is anything which can usefully
be said to the correspondent and action accordingly.

(c) - No acknowledgement has been sent. In this case,

however, it is obviously important that both an
acknowledgement and a full reply are sent.

Unless specifically asked to da so, there is no need for you to copy your
replies to this office.

A new Open Government Code of Practice came into force in January 1997.
All replies to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
ect Mo 48/97; further information is available from DOMD on extension

|

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep record of
their performance. All branches and| Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the
accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be performed
throughout the year.

From the Assistant Private Secretary . 13 October 1997

Dear Correspondence Secretary,
PRIME MINISTER’S OFFICE CORRESPONDENCE SECTION

We have a regrettable and unprecedented backlog of some eight weeks’
correspondence here. We are doing what we can to rectify this but are making little
inroad into it - mainly due to the Prime Minister’s postbag being double that of his
predecessor.

To this end we shall be forwarding letters on to departments in the normal manner
but, until further notice, we shall not be acknowledging nor recording any letters which
come to you from this office for treat official action from today on, unless notified to the
contrary. I would be very grateful therefore, if you would ensure that a reply is always
sent on our behalf. What this may mean for your department is that you receive a larger
batch than normal in the initial stages of this exercise. | You will be receiving those letters
received here in both August and September. Please deal with them in date order as far
as possible. The folders which we send to your department will be clearly marked as to
the dates received in this office.

The Prime Minister is very keen that his correspondence is replied to as quickly as
possible and I would be very grateful if you could try, within your own probably stretched
resources, to meet his request.

Can I please ask you to ensure that all those within your department who deal with
Prime Minister’s treat official correspondence are made aware of this temporary
arrangement.

——__¥ sincerel

The Correspondence Secretary

“1. Thank you for your letter of 29 October in which you have
requested information on several “unexplained” sightings.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines

any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
-. establish whether what was seen might have some defence

significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK

Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
- unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not

¥. attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
‘to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. I have looked back through our sighting report files and can

confirm that no sightings were reported to the Ministry of Defence
which tie in with the dates and locations specified in your

letter.
Yous sinc,

“1. Thank you for your letter in which you have requested
information on "unidentified flying objects". This office is the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence of
this nature.

2B, First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence

’ significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
‘Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. -The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.
“BUS SVCONOLY,,

Section 4 Z. December 1997

1. Thank-you for your letters of 17 October and 20 November
addressed to the Minister of State for the Armed Forces regarding
“unidentified flying objects". Your letters have been passed to
this office -which is the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence of this nature. :

-2. It remains the case that the Ministry of Defence examines any
reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence

“significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK

_Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat we do
not attempt to identify the precise nature of each report. We
believe that down to earth explanations could be found for these
reports, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, if
resources were diverted for this purpose but it would be an
inappropriate use of defence resources to provide this kind of
aerial identification service.

4. Your letters mention Section 40) a former member of
Secretariat (Air Staff) 2. I. should point out that the views
expressed by Section 40] in ‘connection with "unexplained" aerial
sightings are his personal opinions and do not represent or
reflect the views of the MOD.

Yours SQV,

Please send a reply on behplf of the Minister concerned. All
Ministers attach importance to such letters being answered promptly,
your reply should therefore be sent within 20 working days of the date

A new Open Government Code of Practice on Access to
Government Information came into force in January 1997. All replies
to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained
in DCI(Gen) 48/97: further information is available from DOMD on
i tcnsio

|

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep
records of their performance. All branches and Agencies are required
to keep information on the number of requests for information which
refer to the Code of Practice including details of the correspondent and
the nature and date of the reply. In addition, the Department is required
to provide a record of the total number of letters from members of the
public and provide statistics (which may be based on a valid sample) of
its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on

the accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be
performed throughout the year.

Thank you for your letter regarding two sightings of . unusual
aerial activity over London. I am sorry for the delay in replying.

oa I have looked back through our sighting report files and can
confirm that the Ministry of Defence did not receive any similar
reports from the London area on either 21 or 22 September 1997.

35 As I explained in my letter to you of 30 June, unless there
is evidence to suggest a matter of defence significance, i.e. that
the UK Air Defence Region may have been breached by hostile or
unauthorised foreign military activity, the MOD does not seek to
provide a precise explanation for each sighting reported to us.

ection 40, . 24 November 1997

Thank you for your letter of 23 November to the Under
secretary of State for Defence in which you have requested
information on “unidentified flying objects" for your Media and
Business degree course. Your letter has been passed: to this office
which is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence of this nature. I have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "“UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.
5. Finally, your letter refers to a former member
of staff in this office (Secretariat (Air Staff) 2). The views

expressed by on the subject of "UFOs" are his personal
opinions and do not represent or reflect the views of the MOD.

‘ors Siardy,

Dear Mr Spellar,

We are 3rd year students studying for a University of Manchester degree in Media and
Business. For our television production class we are producing a documentary

programme about UFO investigations.

We are writing to you for information about the involvement of the Ministry of
Defence in such studies. We would particularly like to know about any recent UFO
sightings which have been reported to the government. Such issues obviously
concern certain sectors of the electorate and we would like to know what action the
government takes in response these concerns and whether the Ministry of Defence

takes seriously any reports of UFO sightings it receives.

We understand Megsection 40 | has in the past been assigned to UFO investigations
for the government and we would be very grateful if you could give us any
information about the job which he did, or indeed supply us with an address where we

could contact him.

Thanking you for you help with this important project.

Yours sincerely,

1. Thank you for your letter of 6 November.

2. Perhaps it would help clarify matters if I set out once more
the MOD's policy regarding reports of “unidentified flying
objects". Reports are examined solely to establish whether what
was seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether
there is any evidence that the UK Air Defence Region might have
been compromised by hostile or unauthorized foreign military
activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat we do
not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting
reported to us. We believe that down to earth explanations could
be found for these reports if resources were diverted for this
purpose but I am afraid it would be an inappropriate use of
defence resources to provide this kind of aerial identification

service.

Your ref, D/Sec(AS)/64/3

Dear

Thank you for your reply dated 3rd November.

I can appreciate that you have more information regarding low flying aircraft
movements, because of ‘booking’ procedures, than those of high altitude.
However, | do find it difficult to understand that the MOD is unable to confirm
or deny if any RAF or other foreign aircraft (friendly or otherwise) was in
British airspace at a given time, location, date and approximate altitude.

If you are suggesting that too much effort would be required to look into this
matter, then with respect, IT would like to remind you that several people who
witnessed this aerial activity are quite concerned as to what it was and the
implications that such unusual activity might mean.

T do feel that it would misrepresent the MOD to members of the public who
have a genuine concern, by informing them of the disproportionate effort
required to allay their anxiety.

I thank you for your concern in this matter.

Yours sincerely

The attached letter(s) which the Prime Minister has received has been
forwarded to this Department for official action. No.10's letter codes are as
follows:

A - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
send a full reply within 20 working days.

B - The Jetter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
consider whether there is anything which can usefully
be said to the correspondent and action accordingly.

- No acknowledgement has been sent. In this case,
however, it is obviously important that both an
acknowledgement and a full reply are sent.

Unless specifically asked to do so. there is no need for you to copy your
replies to this office.

A new Open Government Code of Practice came into force in January 1997.
All replies to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
ection 40) 48/97; further information is available from DOMD on extension

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep record of
their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the
accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be performed witty.

1. Thank you for your letter of 21 September addressed to the
Prime Minister regarding “unidentified flying objects". Your
letter has been passed to the Ministry of Defence and this office
is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence of this
nature. I am sorry for the delay in replying.

2 First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines.
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial Jlifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

5. Your comments relating to the alleged events in Roswell in
1947 are a matter for the United States Government and are not for
the UK MOD to remark upon. Should you wish to pursue your query
with the US Government, you may write to the US Embassy at 24
Grosvenor Square, London, W1A I1AE.

From the Assistant Private Secretary 13 October 1997

Dear Correspondence Secretary,
PRIME MINISTER’S OFFICE CORRESPONDENCE SECTION

We have a regrettable and unprecedented backlog of some eight weeks’
correspondence here. We are doing what we can to rectify this but are making little
inroad into it - mainly due to the Prime Minister’s postbag being double that of his
predecessor.

To this end we shall be forwarding letters on to departments in the normal manner
but, until further notice, we shall not be acknowledging nor recording any letters which
come to you from this office for treat official action from today on, unless notified to the
contrary. I would be very grateful therefore, if you would ensure that a reply is always
sent on our behalf. What this may mean for your department is that you receive a larger
batch than normal in the initial stages of this exercise. | You will be receiving those letters
received here in both August and September. Please deal with them in date order as far
as possible. The folders which we send to your department will be clearly marked as to
the dates received in this office.

The Prime Minister is very keen that his correspondence is replied to as quickly as
possible and I would be very grateful if you could try, within your own probably stretched
resources, to meet his request.

Can I please ask you to ensure that all those within your department who deal with
Prime Minister’s treat official correspondence are made aware of this temporary
arrangement.

Yours sincerel

The Correspondence Secretary

Ly am writing with reference to your message left on the
Secretariat (Air Staff) answerphone in which you reported an
"unidentified flying object" seen on 31 October 1997. This office
is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence of this nature.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4, With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 31
October in or around North Yorkshire, and we are satisfied that
there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

Yours sinew tly, foyeve bio ow

FIED FLYING
NUTTER

arry had always wanted

to fly, and even joined
the Air Force hoping to
become a pilot, only to
meet with dissapointment
because of his poor eye-
sight. To fulfil his dream he
popped down to his local
Army-Navy store and pur-
chased several tanks of heli-
um gas and 45 weather bal-
loons. After filling the bal-
loons, attatching them to a
lawn chair which was
anchored to his jeep, Larry
grabbed some sandwiches
and his pellet gun (in order
to pop the balloons), and
cut the anchor.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Larry had planned on
reaching around 30 feet, the
problera was that he finally
leveled off at 11000 feet. And
there he stayed for fourteen
hours, too frightened to pop
the balloons.

Prob.ems really started
when drifted into the primary
approach corridor of LA
international airport. The
tower received a report from
an astonished captain of a guy
in a lawn chair waving a gun
at him and immediately
launched a helicopter to
investigate.

Soon it was determined
that Larry was not dangerous
and a rescue was attempted, a
rope was lowerered and Larry
was towed back to safety and
arrested. He later said "A man
can't just sit around."

JK: What's your gut feel-
ing, Nick, regarding just how
much the government really
knows about the UFO phe-
nomenon?

NR: One of the things I
found from examining the
files at the PRO, and some of
the military files which have
been declassified from
America which concern
British involvement in the
subject, is that there isn't
really one office which gets to

see all the information.

If you write to the Ministry
of Defence you will get a let-
ter from the Secretary to the
Air Staff, which is the office
in which Nick Pope worked
and which Kerry Philpott is
responsible for now. Their
opinion, or their viewpoint, is
that there is no evidence that
UFOs are extraterrestrial, or
that they represent any
advanced technology, and
there's no evidence that they
represent a threat to the
defence of the realm. By their
own admission, too, a good
95-96% of all the reports
which enter their office come
from the public. They maybe
get a few reports from the
police, and the odd military

report, which usually comes

from a base PR officer.

What I've done in my
book, I've quoted on-the-
record testimonies of several
people (ex-military) who've
told me that, although those
reports go to Whitehall, the
good-quality pilot reports
and radar reports go to
Rudloe Manor. So Rudloe
Manor gets the meat of the
reports. Whitehall's public
office gets the reports from
the general public, so you've
got the left hand really not
knowing what the right hand
is doing.

So when people say, 'Is the
MoD covering this up; is the
MoD covering that up?’ ...
you've got a situation where
behind-the-scenes high-level
investigations are being car-
ried out and they are taking

the subject very, very serious-

ly.

But ... as far as the ques-
tion of what they know ... I'm
convinced that as far back as
the early 1950s, when the
official records state that
pilots and so on were ordered
not to talk about sightings -
and that's officially in the
recorcls - I think they recog-
nized that strange things were
going on. These objects were
entering our airspace, and we
didn't have a lot of control

over the situation.

Having said that, I'm not
aware of any official reports
where a UFO has, say,
downed a British fighter-
plane. But I think they know
that something strange is
going on. I don't think
they've got all the answers -
they may not have half the

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

answers, but I think the fact
that these things are able to
come and go and our govern-
ments and the military aren't
able to do anything about it
suggests that the MoD have
taken a stance whereby
they're going to deny that
there's anything to the sub-
ject. They can't be seen to
admit that our airspace can
be intruded upon and they
can't do anything about it. So
I think they took the decision
to deny that there is anything
really to the subject.

But, again, as the official
records are being released, it
shows that contrary to public
opinion, they did take these
things seriously in the '40s,
‘50s and '60s; that's proven by
the official records. I've no
doubt that when the records
are released from the '70s,
80s and '90s they'll show
exactly the same thing.

JK: So, Nick, the sixty-
four-thousand-dollar ques-
tion - is there a genuine
extraterrestrial phenomenon
occurring in our skies?

NR: Oh yes. As far as I'm
concerned, yes. I think when
you've got a good solid
report, say from a military
pilot or radar operator, or a
civil pilot, those people have
got a hell of a lot to lose by
filing a false report, and
they're the people trained to
know what goes on in the
skies, recognizing cloud for-
mations, aircraft landing, all
this type of thing. I think
when you've got a report
from a quality source like
that, describing a disc-shaped
object travelling at high
speed, you have to accept

their testimony, and I don't
know of anything which we
operated in the 40s, 50s, and
60s which could travel 2,000
miles an hour plus and do

left and right hand turns.
These reports aren't simply of
balls of light in the sky; these
are constructed objects, and
my considered opinion, based
on the research and the files,
is that a small percentage are
extraterrestrial.

And I'm convinced that our
government knows that, and
has taken a stance to officially

deny that that's the case.

I also think there is some
collaboration with the
Americans. Again, this is
borne out by the files to some
degree. There are indications
in some of the files which
have been released that the
Americans have discussed the
subject with the British on a
number of occasions, as far
back as '47 and at least as
late as '92. That suggests that
if the Roswell event was gen-
uine, which I think it was, it
could well be the case that we
have been receiving updated
briefings on the subject since
that time. And as I say, this is
borne out by the files.

So I'm convinced the gov-
ernment knows there's an
extraterrestrial element, and I
also think it's only a matter of
time before this information
surfaces into the public
domain.

JK: Thank you, Nick. And
good luck with the release of

your book,

NR: My pleasure.

I am writing ia response to your letter of 30 August in which you ask
about the involvement in the Apollo space program by one of our dase
organizations. In the late 1960s and early 1970s, the 67th Aerospace
Rescue and Recovery Squadron stationed at RAF Woodbridge did indeed
provide standby rescue coverage for the American space flights. Of
course, they were never needed to provide emergency rescue actions, but
at the time, the unit was trained and available cto rescue astronauts
with their HH-53 and HC-130 aircrafe.

in early§968, the 67 ARRS was redesignated as part of the 39th Special
Operations Wing, their primary mission changing from that of rescue

to supporting J.S. Special Operarions forces. Their secondary mission
remains that of search and rescue and they would provide any assistance
necessary in future space missions. lowever, I have no decails on their
plans to do so.

Thank you very much for your interesc in the United States Air Force.

ARE ALL CROP
FORMATIONS
HOAXED by

KEVIN |
OLLIER

nformation has recently come our way regard-
iE a forthcoming UFO and crop circle pro-

gramme to be aired on Channel 4 (1998). We
have been told that the nature of this programme
is largely to debunk these two phenomena, in par-
ticular the crop circles - it seems the Doug and
Dave scam has finally died an inglorious death and
the government has thus been forced to invent a
new and more convincing attempt to discredit this
phenomenon in: the publi eye.

With this in mind we asked Kevin Ollier to come up
with a few facts, which he did...

Of Course They Are!

When one begins to get interested in crop circles, like it or
not, sooner probably than later one has to ask the question:
"Are all crop formations hoaxed?" Mainstream science, the
media, government and the military will say: "Of course they
are!"

In 1991 Doug and Dave claimed that they began the
whole thing - "just having fun with the UFO boys", they
were quoted as saying. What they didn't do was done by.”
copycat hoaxers.

In fact I recently spoke to Doug Bower on the phone:
Doug still stands by this claim. He thinks the "phenomena"
still continues because in his words "too many people are
making money from it for it to stop". I asked Doug: whether
he could entertain the thought that, whilst he and Dave were
stomping the fields, there was a real phenomenon in progress
simultaneously, Doug just giggled!

I personally do not know (or care) whether D & D did one
circle; ten circles or.all those:that they claimed. I, for myself,
know that.a real phenomenon exists, as I have had: my own
proof, which at the end of the day is what any individual”
would like. But it should be obvious to everybody who. has
ever truly studied the phenomenon that there is more to this
than Doug and-Dave.

UFO REALITY + PAGE 46

2s.
20% Maximum
Sure, there are hoaxers and hoaxing gangs: But my esti-
mate is that 20% maximum is their tally. of all formations

ever made in the UK.

' Disregarding the sherianigans surrounding the D & Dera,

i; with conspiracy theories, unknown sources, et¢., etc., there is

still enough evidence, some strong, some trivial, to prove that
these glyphs are more than rollers, planks and telescopic
rods.

The 1980s’ crop circle guru, Colin Andrews, when inter-
viewed in UFO Reality, Issues 2 and,3,.told of how he was
offered a Swiss bank account by the CIA to publicly state
that the crop circle phenomenon was-a hoax. It was also in
these articles that Colin revealed how he discovered that Jim
Schnabel and Rosemary Guiley were working for the US
Government (CIA). Not the sort of people to be involved
witha phenomenon which is purely the result of hoaxes.

Next, there is the military interest, although denied. Video
footage has been taken by several people of army helicopters
hovering over formations and even buzzing the camera oper-
ators and flying very low - illegally - over public roads.
Croppies have been harassed by, or have had’strange ~~"
encounters with, military personnel in crop circle land, espe-
cially in the early hours of the ‘morning.

Strange Lights

Strange lights and sounds have been recorded at or near
crop formations. The famous trilling sound was recorded by
the BBC, who then experienced equipment failure. Many
people report hearing this noise, which is beyond the

" grasshopper, warbler or crow-scarer explanations, although
‘these are also heard.

There have:been literally hundreds:of reports of light phe-
nomena; but some actually filmed. Probably the most inter-
esting was Steve Alexander's footage showing a ball of light
skipping over a field and then heading towards a distant trac-
tor. Later the tractor driver verified the film, describing. what
he had seen coming towards him! Other films. such as the
Gérman students' and Colin Andrews’ are all evidence that —
weird lights are seen, and mostly in broad daylight.

As regards the Oliver's Castle footagé (showing small balls

' _ of white light apparently creating a crop formation) my per-

sonal view is that it is hoaxed.J think this footage was made
because the likes of Steve Alexander's film showed real phe-
nomena, but-a suspicious film like‘ the Oliver's Castle one
could make all films seem dubious. For me; the Oliver's
Castle footage was made deliberately to cast doubt on the
likes of Steve Alexander's genuine footage.

Then there is the small matter of the scientific evidence,
which does exist, mostly thanks to Dr Levengood's work:in
the USA: enlargement of cell pits, abnormal expansion of

1. I am writing with reference to your recent report of an
unexplained aerial sighting which you observed on 6 November 1997.
The details of your report have been passed from RAF Leuchars to
this office as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence relating to "unidentified flying
objects".

2. First perhaps it would be useful if I were to explain the
MOD's interest with respect to reports of "unidentified flying
objects". The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of "UFOs"

it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence
that the UK Air Defence Region might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4: With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no similar reports of "UFO" sightings for 6
November in the Dundee area, and we are satisfied that there is no
corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
Significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

do so.

1. Thank you for your letter of 17 September regarding the
alleged incident at Rendlesham Forest. :

2. You have questioned whether the Ministry of Defence
thoroughly investigated the events which are alledged to have
taken place at RAF Woodbridge/Rendlesham Forest. From Departmental
records available for the period in question, we have established
that all available information was looked at at the time by air
defence experts who were satisfied that nothing had occurred to
suggest that the UK Air Defence Region had been breached by
unauthorised foreign military activity on the nights in question.
In the absence of evidence corroborating Col Halt's memo, which
was sent some two weeks after the events in 1980, and in the light
of the Department's air defence remit, no action was then deemed
necessary. It was then, and still is, the case that the Department
does not routinely contact witnesses following receipt of a report
of an "unexplained" aerial sighting. Follow-up action is only
deemed to be required if there is corroborating evidence of a
matter of air defence interest.

3. I should like to assure you that Defence technology,
including the effectiveness of our air defence systems, is
constantly evolving and we are confident that our air defence
capabilities fully meet the air defence threat and protect the
integrity of the UK Air Defence Region.

17 September 1997

Was British Nuclear Base Under Hostile Threat?

Thank you for your letter dated 11 September 1997.

I was somewhat surprised that a letter addressing a defence related matter had been
directed to an office of the Ministry which focused on “the ‘UFO’ phenomenon”.
Such terminology was used nowhere in my letter and tends to presuppose certain
connotations which I do not attribute to the matter at hand.

Nonetheless, I recognise your knowledge of the case and am sincerely grateful for the
information provided.

You state that “all available substantiated evidence was looked at in the usual manner
by those within the MOD/RAF with responsibility for air defence matters”.

However, Colonel Halt, the Deputy Base Commander who had informed the Ministry

of the reported incidents, contends that the Ministry did not apparently investigate his
report.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

For instance, during a videotaped interview, he claims, “I sent the memo, but I never
heard. I kept checking and asking but I never got a response”.

“To this day I’m very puzzled why no-one ever came back and asked for additional
information, asked questions, or even interviewed me. It doesn’t really add up”.

Of particular importance, he has also confirmed, “There are a lot of things that are not
in my memo, but there was no response from the Ministry of Defence so I didn’t go
any further with them”.

In other words, Colonel Halt asserts that, to his knowledge, the Ministry did not
investigate the official report, most certainly never contacted him to discuss it and as a
result, the Ministry was not aware of all that transpired.

As the Ministry and the RAF did not apparently seek to interview Colonel Halt, or
any of the key witnesses concerned with the reported incidents, how can the Ministry
claim that “all available substantiated evidence was looked at”?

Could you please confirm what evidence the Ministry and RAF did in fact look at.

I would also like to formally request copies of any relevant documentation, for
example, correspondence, minutes of meetings and any final report, which
demonstrate the nature and scope of the Ministry and RAF’s investigation.

You express the view that, “nothing has emerged over the last 16% years which has
given us reason to believe that the original assessment made by this Department was
incorrect”.

I am pleased to inform the Ministry that new evidence has now been disclosed and
perhaps the time has come for the public to ask that your Department’s confidence be
reassessed, or explanations offered for the disparity we see between the Ministry’s
stance and that of the apparent witnesses.

I enclose for the Ministry’s information the transcript of an interview recently given
by Staff Sergeant Jim Penniston, one of the three security police patrolmen referred to
in the first paragraph of Halt’s official report.

In a solemn and detailed testimony, revealing previously unknown information,
Penniston alleges that an object of unknown origin was corroborated on radar by RAF
Bawdsey, that an object which had apparently landed was then discovered in
Rendlesham Forest, that it was witnessed by base personnel, examined in detail by the
Law Enforcement officer and that it’s appearance was documented, in addition to 36
photographs having been taken.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

I also enclose copies of sketches, never before released, which Staff Sergeant
Penniston claims originate from his notebook and which he dutifully recorded as he
surveyed the anomalous craft.

I further enclose the transcript of a recent testimony from Colonel Charles Halt, in
which he confirms that two nights later, unidentified aerial objects, directed laser-like
beams of light near the weapons storage area.

He also elaborates on the process which led to the incidents being discussed at a
meeting of the Third Air Force and which ultimately led to the decision that the entire
matter fell within the remit of the Ministry to investigate.

Which seems to bring us full circle and begs the question; exactly how thorough was
the Ministry and RAF’s subsequent investigation.

Was Halt’s official report possibly deemed not worthy of more than superficial
investigation, such that we now find ourselves in a situation where a member of the
public is providing you with evidence which should have perhaps have been
addressed some 17 years ago?

1 believe that further new testimonies from former base personnel, including affidavits
from new witnesses, will also be published in the near future.

Your comments concerning the Ministry’s attitude towards, “flying saucers” and ET
lifeforms are noted and I am grateful to be thus enlightened.

I would however respectfully point out that I seek not the answer to such questions,
but demonstrable proof that the Ministry has fulfilled its duty to investigate the
serious allegations made by ostensibly highly credible members of the USAF, who
were responsible for the security of a major NATO facility on British soil.

I have now provided some of the evidence, much of which may be new to you, that
appears to call into question whether the Ministry did adequately investigate the
incidents summarised in Colonel Halt’s report.

I should be grateful if the Ministry could now please provide some actual evidence to
the contrary.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Testimony from Staff-Sergeant Jim Penniston
81%* Security Police Squadron, RAF Bentwaters/RAF Woodbridge

Abridged from an article by journalist AJS Rayl

"There is some confusion about the date. There are two duty rosters,
both of which are dated December 26th, but it was either that night,
the 26th, or the 27th. I reported for work as usual, and at about 6
pm, I prepared for guard mount. The men reported in to Bentwaters
around 7 pm.

"From Bentwaters, we transported over to RAF Woodbridge [Bentwaters'
British counterpart base] in a metro on the back road. It was just
business as usual. Since it was just after the Christmas holiday, we
expected it to be a slow, quiet night.

"About 12:02 am -- I remember that distinctly -- I was dispatched to
the East Gate, which was sometimes referred to as the back gate at
Woodbridge. There, I was told to contact Police 2, which was Airman
First Class John Burroughs and Staff Sgt. Bud Steffans, and that I
would be briefed when I got there.

"Upon arriving at the East Gate, Staff Sgt. Steffans told me that
there was a problem out in the woods. I gazed about 300 meters into
the distance and saw what appeared to be a fire. My first impression
was that we had an aircraft down. I saw orange, red, and bluish types
of glowing light, pretty standard with aircraft crashes. I asked Staff
Sgt. Steffans if he had heard it go down. He told me there had been

no sound, that it didn't crash, that it had landed. I discounted that
and, after assessing in my own mind what to do, decided we should call
it in to our superiors at Bentwaters Center for Security Control
(CSC).

"IT went to the gate shack, phoned in, and spoke with Master Sergeant
J.D. Chandler, who was the on-duty flight chief for both bases. He
asked me to stand by while he contacted the Control Tower at
Bentwaters and also at Woodbridge. While I was talking with Chandler,
the security controller was making contact with Bentwaters Control
Tower, and they, in turn, were in contact with Heathrow Airport's
Control Tower in London and RAF Bawdsey Eastern Radar in East Anglia.
I was told they reported a bogie about three miles out from Bentwaters
but had lost contact with it 15 minutes previously. The confirmation
that triangulated the radar sighting was RAF Bawdsey. It had been over
Woodbridge, they told me, when it dropped from radar imaging.

"TI was still pretty sure it was an aircraft downing at that point and
asked Master Sgt. Chandler for permission to investigate. Master Sgt.
Chandler contacted the shift commander's office, and within a minute
or so I got the go-ahead to proceed off base with two other security
policemen. We were told to leave our weapons behind, so as to not
violate the Status Forces Agreement with the British. I and Airman
First Class John Burroughs and Ed Cabansag, also an Airman First
Class, got into our Jeep and proceeded out the East Gate, then down

a logging road adjacent to the perimeter. It got too rocky, so we had
to dismount the vehicle.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"Our radios -- the standard Motorola type -- were experiencing a
dampening effect common with atmospheric conditions. Basically they
were breaking up and I was not able to send or recover full
transmissions. At that point, I decided to station Airman Cabansag
there as a radio link, so he stayed by the Jeep to serve as a
communications relay. Airman Burroughs and I then headed on foot
toward the tree line, approximately 50 meters away, beyond which was
a clearing -- which is where the activity, the lights were coming
from."

"As we started getting closer, it was apparent that it was not an
aircraft downing or a crash. We weren't sure what it was at that
point, so we radioed that information back to Airman Cabansag, who
radioed it back to CSC. We had no outside contact with our IBRs
[radios], with the exception of Airman Cabansag, and he was, by this
point, beginning to break up on the transmissions himself.

"We entered the tree line and moved in about 20 more meters. There was
an object sitting in a clearing. It was emitting mostly white light

at that point, very bright. Both Burroughs and I had to squint when

we looked at it. I found it very strange that there was no sound
coming from the object, but the animals around us were in a frenzy.

We had wildlife running by us, and lots of birds. Outside that noise,
however, there was no other.

"That was when I decided to have Burroughs stay there as a radiolink.
He did not seem calm. He didn't acknowledge what I was saying, but I
thought he understood. I was more concerned at that moment with what
was going on in front of me.

"As I continued on into the woods, I started seeing the outline of the
object itself. The lights that had just been a blur from 300 meters
away were now definite, distinct colors -- light blue, yellow, and red
-- and they were pulsating. Looking at the silhouette of the object,

I realized it was not a conventional aircraft, meaning that it was not
one that was already published in Jane's All the World's Aircraft. It
was no aircraft I had ever seen, and it wasn't one that I knew any
prototype of.

"I had my notebook and camera while I was out there, so I began taking
notes. This is what I wrote:

Triangular in shape. The top portion is producing mainly white light,
which encompasses most of the upper section of the craft. A small
amount of white light peers out the bottom. At the left side center
is a bluish light, and on the other side, red. The lights seem to be
molded as part of the exterior of the structure, smooth, slowly
fading into the rest of the outside of the structure, gradually
molding into the fabric of the craft.

"As I was taking notes, I also memorized what was in front of me for
what seemed like hours, but was in fact only minutes. Finally, I
unleashed my camera-case cover and brought the camera up to focus. The
air was electric. It made my hair and skin feel as if I were
surrounded by static electricity or some type of energy. I began
snapping photo after photo. It was still eerily quiet."
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"IT got to within 10 feet of the craft and the clearing where it sat.
I estimated it to be about three meters tall and about three meters
wide at the base. No landing gear was apparent, but it seemed like she
was on fixed legs. I moved a little closer. I had already taken all
36 pictures on my roll of film. I walked around the craft, and
finally, I walked right up to the craft. I noticed the fabric of the
shell was more like a smooth, opaque, black glass. The bluish lights
went from black to gray to blue. I was pretty much confused at that
point. I kept trying to put this in some kind of frame of reference,
trying to find some logical explanation as to what this was and what
was going on. It was dead silent. No animals were even making noise
anymore.

"On the smooth exterior shell there was writing of some kind, but I
couldn't quite distinguish it, so I moved up to it. It was three-inch
lettering, rather symbols that stretched for the length of two feet,
maybe a little more. I touched the symbols, and I could feel the
shapes as if they were inscribed or etched or engraved, like a diamond
cut on glass.

"At that point, I backed away from the craft, because the light was
starting to get brighter. Still, there was no sound. There was no
physical contact with any kind of life form, but there did seem to be
a life presence. It was mechanical, this ship, and it seemed to be
under intelligent control.

"The next thing I knew, I was standing about 20 feet away from the
craft with Burroughs, who I thought I had left back near the tree
line. The craft moved up off the ground, about three feet, still with
absolutely no sound. It started to move slowly, weaving back through
the trees at a very slow pace, maybe a half a foot per second. It took
about a couple of minutes for it to maneuver itself back to a

distance of about 100 to 150 feet, then it rose up just over the
trees, about 200 feet high. There was a momentary pause -- and then
literally with the blink of an eye it was gone. All with no sound.
That still boggles my mind.

"We thought it had left, but then both Airman Burroughs and I saw the
same array of colored lights maybe a half mile away. So we pursued it,
trying to follow its course as best we could on foot. We only got
about 300 yards into the woods before we turned around. We still had
no radio contact, which I thought was strange. We weren't even getting
squelch. We went back to the clearing. There, Airman Burroughs noticed
the impression, the indentations in the ground. We found three of
them, all triangular in shape, each about three meters apart. Then I
decided we should head back."
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"When we arrived at CSC [Bentwaters Center for Security Control], we
ran into Sgt. Chandler and two or three other security people. They
had had negative contact with us for almost three hours, and they had
been concerned. I remember saying to Sgt. Chandler, “You're not going
to believe tonight.' He said, “Yeah? If it has anything to do with
what I saw a little while ago, I would believe you.'

"Airman Burroughs and I were then instructed to report to the shift
commander's office. The assistant operations officer was there to
debrief us. As we stood at attention in his office, he spoke to us in
a very steady, calm voice. He said, “Can you gentlemen explain to me
what happened out there tonight?' So we explained, very briefly. We
didn't tell him any specifics about the symbols or the design of the
craft.

"After a very long pause, and very calmly, while he was tapping his
pencil on the table, he said, “Gentlemen, what you say you experienced
tonight is no longer able to be reported through Air Force channels.'
He then gave us something of a history lesson on Project Blue Book and
that it was terminated in 1969. Basically, he told us that there was
no official way to report this up. Then he said, “Some things are best
left unsaid.' He asked us to keep quiet about it, to forget it
happened.

"That mentality, that mindset and thought process, also caused the
security controller to delete it from the blotter and retype the
blotter entry with a minimal description, something to the effect of
“Investigated aircraft crash off base' and three or four brief
sentences following. Also, an Air Force Form 1569, an accident and
complaint report, was filled out. Later, I learned that that was what
stimulated Lt. Col. Halt, when he reviewed the blotter the next
morning and after hearing about what really had happened, to insist
something about it be put into the blotter."

"After the debriefing, Airman Burroughs and I were put on authorized
break for six days, so we drove home to Ipswich. I dropped Airman
Burroughs off, then went home, changed clothes, and drove back to
Woodbridge. But first I stopped by a friend's place in Ipswich, who
was a contractor and painter, who gave me some plaster. Then, I went
back out to the forest and the clearing where the three indentations
left by the craft we had seen were.

"T poured plaster into the impressions left in the ground by the craft
and waited about 40 minutes. Then I pulled them up and put them in the
back of my car, just as Maj. Ed Drurry, the Deputy Security Police
Commander, and the assistant operations officer showed up. They asked
me what I was doing. I told them I was just looking around. They told
me they wanted to do the same.
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"T didn't tell them about making the casts, because I didn't think it
would be prudent. Later I found out that the deputy base commander,
then-Lieutenant Colonel Halt, had also visited the site later that
same day and noticed that there were traces of plaster around the
impressions, the indentations, and later, much later, I finally
admitted to him what I had done. I: needed something for myself,
something to prove to me that this had really happened, really
physically happened.

"The interesting thing about the impressions, or something worthy of
note, anyway, is that the ground temperature at the time was such that
our vehicles didn't even make impressions on the ground because it was
so cold or frozen, so whatever the craft was, it had to be heavy.

"I had dropped my film off at the base lab for developing, but I never
got them back. I never saw them. I was just told that they didn't turn
out. I didn't understand that but was not in a position to push the
issue. The cameras we carried in our vehicles were good ones, used to
photograph people on the perimeter. We were under a high terrorist
threat at the time with the Irish Republican Army (IRA), another
terrorist group known as Black September, and some others, so we used
them frequently as part of our patrol."

"Two days later, there was another, second encounter. I did not go out
on that night. Lt. Col. Halt was sent out to investigate.

After that incident, however, I was directed to report to OSI [Office
of Special Investigations] at 0900 in the morning. I met with a couple
of agents, whom I had known because they had an office on the base.
They debriefed me for about an hour and a half about the incident. It
was an oral debriefing where I basically just told them what had
happened, and they seemed quite content with the information that I
provided them at the time. They seemed to have no problem with the
fact that I had seen a craft".

"In the meantime, Lt. Col. Halt issued his memorandum to the British
Ministry of Defense. I do not find it odd that he didn't get any
response, for the simple reason that there was no official way of
reporting this kind of thing and so there was no official way for
anyone to react to the upchanneling of information. And quite frankly,
I don't think anyone suspected or expected the deputy base commander
to shoot off a memo to MoD. I think OSI thought the information was
contained, because no written report had been taken up through U.S.
channels and the debriefings were all taken care of."

"Tt was a difficult time after the incident. For years afterward, I
would hear the stories within military circles and I never commented
on it in any way at all. Nor did I ever speak to any UFO researchers
or members of the media. Still, I noted with some disconcertment all
of the untruths being discussed, especially in the media starting in
the mid-eighties after the Halt memo was released through the Freedom
of Information Act. I read accounts quoting people who claimed to be
primary witnesses -- and they weren't -- saying certain events
happened, which they didn't.
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"I was extremely upset by the memo's release, because the story being
leaked as a result included my name and I was being tied into it. I
had been assured by my senior officers at Bentwaters that at no time
would my name or whatever be used or released outside official U.S.
channels. Of course, they hadn't released it, but the next thing I
know, it's being covered on CNN and Unsolved Mysteries and in books
and magazines. Fortunately, I was still in the service and managed to
duck them all. But I was confused. I thought -- and I had been told
-- that this was a top secret incident. And now ali this.

"I had no interest in anything like this before 1980. I always thought
that anybody who had seen strange lights or claimed to have seen UFOs
in the sky was crazy. I am a logical person. Even to this day, I am
still trying to rationalize from previous experience what that craft
was, what happened. But there it is: I mean, I am standing out in the
clearing in the middle of the forest and there is this craft, clearly
triangular in shape, looking like nothing I have ever seen or ever
heard about, and it doesn't make any sound, and it's got lights, and

I walk around it and take pictures of it and I even touch it, and
nobody, no country, is claiming it. I am 39 percent certain of one
thing and that is, this craft was definitely not of USAF origin or at
least not that I know of, and nobody I know has ever heard anything
about a craft like this then or now. And what other country might have
such a craft that has those capabilities of maneuvering in tight
conditions in the middle of a forest and all with no sound? That
disturbed me then, and it disturbs me now."

"In a conversation with Colonel Halt in 1990 [he was now a full
Colonel], I asked him about the top secret status of all this in light
of what had happened. He told me then that there was nothing
classified about any of this. The Air Force, you see, has always had
this sort of policy that, ‘Yeah, you can talk about this incident or
that incident because technically it isn't classified, not officially
classified, but -- it's not a good idea.' That was always the way it
had been given to me. So I never talked to anybody about this.
However, others have, including some who don't know what they're
talking about because they weren't out there. There's a lot of truth
to be told here, and that's all anybody wants to hear is the facts.
To sensationalize it, well . . . that's pointless. It's pretty
sensational in and of itself what happened.

"For starters, this is one of the most witnessed and best documented
UFO encounters ever, observed from a distance by some 80 people on the
first night and an additional 30 on the second night -- all of whom
were trained observers. This was our profession -- to be observing
situations that might occur. We were on the alert. Our senses were
ready. Out there, we were trained to be sponges, absorbing everything
we saw or heard and documenting it with notes and mentally. Then there
was my camera and the photographs I took, which have to be somewhere.
There were radio transmissions that were heard and a tape of the
deputy base commander's investigation, which is circulating out there.
Then again, this was an unknown, and there's a lot of fear about the
unknown.

"At this point, I don't think I have broken any confidentiality oaths
with the Department of Defense or the federal government. I would not
do that or anything that would jeopardize the security of our Country."
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Testimony from Colonel Charles Halt
Deputy Base Commander, RAF Bentwaters/RAF Woodbridge

Abridged from an article by journalist AJS Rayl

Decorated repeatedly for meritorious service, Halt is a graduate of
the Air Command and Staff College and the Industrial College of the
Armed Forces. He holds a BS in economics and chemistry (Virginia
Wesleyan, 1962) and an MBA (University of Hawaii, 1975). A specialist
in fighter interceptor aircraft maintenance, he executed a variety of
high-profile assignments with distinction during his 28-year career,
including prestigious air base commands in Asia and Europe, frequently
working in sensitive relationships with foreign air forces. Many of
these security-conscious postings - as well as his promotion to
colonel - occurred after Halt's remarkable report, indicating
substantial Air Force confidence in his credibility, despite the
extraordinary nature of his claims.

. Two nights later, on December 27-28, as Halt and other officers
and their families were about to begin the main course of a belated
officer's Christmas dinner, the on-duty flight security officer
arrived unexpectedly, "white as a sheet." He told Halt and Base
Commander Col. Ted Conrad: 'The UFO is back.' Halt was directed to
investigate.

"Rumors about what Jim Penniston and John Burroughs had seen were
beginning to circulate out of control and I was determined to put an
end to this UFO nonsense," Halt recalls. He headed home to change and
contact a group of men to accompany him into the woods.

It's not that he doubted Penniston or Burroughs. Halt was, he says,
confident that what they had seen was real and - at the very least -
odd. These were, after all, "fine cops" and he could specifically
vouch for Penniston. In fact, he and Base Commander Conrad had decided
to follow-up, but with Christmas, taking their reports slipped to a
lower priority on the agenda. Now this.

Halt and his men stopped by the disaster preparedness office where
they picked up another man and equipment, including a Geiger counter
and camera. Then they headed by jeep into the woods.

On arriving at the edge of the forest, the on-duty flight security
officer handed Halt a starlight scope (infrared scope for nighttime
viewing) and pointed into the woods. "The area was illuminated, almost
as if there were some type of energy," Halt remembers. "I still can't
explain that. But at the time, I just thought there had to be some
logical explanation."

After viewing the strange glow through the starlight scope, Halt
selected five or six men and set out for the clearing where Penniston
and Burroughs had reported the strange, unknown object they saw two
nights before.
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

As they inspected the clearing, Halt began recording verbal
notes into a microcassette recorder, logging the Geiger counter
readings, measurements of the indentations and descriptions of the
apparent damage that appeared on the nearby trees. The disaster
preparedness specialist, who was also a photographer, snapped
pictures.

Intermittently, whenever possible, Halt updated the Command Post on
his radio. Static was a constant. While they had three radio
frequencies connected to three different control centers - the command
post, the base police, and central security control (CSC) -
communication often had to be completed by relay. "That was strange,"
says Halt. "There were dead spots outside the base, but we knew where
those were and they were never in this area before."

Past the clearing and through the forest was a fence, marking the
border of a farm. Suddenly, eerie howlings and cries from the barnyard
animals broke the quiet night. Halt and the other men looked up. A
sun-red glowing object was hovering amongst the trees, pulsing in a
most unusual way.

The ruby-colored orb appeared to have a black center, says Halt, but
past that, he couldn't distinguish a specific shape. It seemed to
about 100 yards away, hovering about 10 to 15 feet off the ground,
slowly maneuvering through the woods. But it was doing something even
more weird than that

. For the few minutes that they watched the glowing red object as
it moved toward the farm, Halt was startled by what appeared to be
flames leaping from the farmer's house. The interior of the house
appeared engulfed. "I didn't know what to think. I was quite
concerned. In fact, we were all quite concerned."

Instinct took over. Halt and his men chased the object up to the
fence, and over into the farmer's field until it ‘exploded' into five
separate objects and disappeared. Halt quickly realized the farmer's
house had not been on fire, that the illusion must have been caused
by the object. As they passed by the house, Halt did a quick
inspection to make sure all was okay but thought better of knocking
on the door. "We were foreigners there, and I thought it prudent to
keep our presence low and under the circumstances unknown, since all
seemed to be okay."

Halt and his men eventually backtracked for evidence. "We looked for
any residue or any evidence that it may have burned, dropped or done
to the trees. We didn't see anything, but keep in mind this was in the
middle of the night and was very dark there. But perhaps, as Jim
Penniston would later report, the strangest thing was the absolute
lack of sound. "I never heard any sound whatsoever, past the animals,
no whirring, humming, buzzing," says Halt. "No sound."

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

The strange events of the night, however, weren't over. As Halt and
company continued on, past the farmer's property, more objects
appeared. Or the same ones reappeared. No one could say for sure.
Whatever they were, they seemed to be under some kind of intelligent
control .

"We've crossed the farmer's field past his house and across the road,
stumbled through a small stream and went out into a large plowed
field. Somebody noticed several objects in the sky to the north -
three objects clearly visible with multiple-colored lights on them.
The objects appeared elliptical and then they turned full round, which
I thought was quite interesting. All three doing that. They were
stationary for awhile and then they started to move at high speed in
sharp angular patterns as though they were doing a grid search. About
that same time, somebody noticed the south, a similar object, it was
round - did not change shape - and at one point it appeared to come
toward us at a very high speed. It stopped overhead and sent down a
small pencil-like beam, sort of like a laser beam. It was an
interesting beam in that it stayed - it was the same size all the way
down the beam. It illuminated the ground about ten feet from us and
we just stood there in awe wondering whether it was a signal, a
warning, or what it was. We really didn't know."

The beam, says Halt, just went away. "It clicked off as though
someone threw a switch, and the object receded, back up into the sky,"
he says. "Then it moved back toward Bentwaters and continued to send
down beams of light, at one point near the weapons storage facility.
We knew that, because we could hear the chatter on the radio."

Halt was baffled. Whatever this object was, wherever it was from, it
must have been under intelligent control. Within a couple of minutes,
it was gone. Perhaps it retreated high into the skies with the other
erratically moving lights up there. It was hard to say.

At around 4:30 a.m., Halt called the investigation off. "It was a cold
winter night," he says. "The wind was blowing, we were wet, and I just
ordered everybody back to the base. I saw no reason to stay out there

any longer. We left those objects up there."

All told, Halt estimates that somewhere between 30 and 40 people
stationed at various places around the dual Bentwaters-—Woodbridge
base, saw the object emitting beams that night. And that meant, says
Halt, that this situation had to be dealt with and contained. "The
thoughts running through my mind were: ‘How am I going to explain
this. Nobody's going to believe this. It's just too ridiculous.' But
it couldn't be ignored, because now too many people were involved."

Fortunately for Halt, his superiors did believe him . A copy of his
microcassette recording was taken by Wing Commander Gordon Williams
to a staff meeting of the Third Air Force. The decision: It was a
British matter. "We were to turn it over to the them," says Halt. So,
on January 13, 1981, after taking and reviewing the reports of then
Staff Sgt. Jim Penniston, and Airmen First Class John Burroughs and
Ed Cabansag, Halt wrote the now-famous one-page memorandum and
cross-channeled it to the British Ministry of Defence (MOD).

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Halt expected to be contacted about the strange events by a British
representative - military or government. There had, after all, a
violation of British airspace. But that didn't happen

What were those objects and where did they come from? Were they
searching for something they left behind? Or for some evidence that
they wanted to destroy, evidence that would prove had been there? Were
they doing reconnaissance work on us? "Immediately down the coast was
RAF Bawdsey, which had done quite a bit of experimental radar work,
and not far from us a secret installation, and further up the coast
was a nuclear power plant. So there were a lot of things that could
have been of interest to somebody else."

Throughout the years, Halt has mulled over and over the possibilities,
considering every conceivable explanation for what they saw, and what
Penniston and Burroughs claim they saw. So far, Halt says, "nothing
works."

Sometimes, Halt appears distressed about not having figured it out by
now. "I gave some thought to the possibility that we had an
experimental craft of some type of hovercraft or stealth object in the
area," he says. "But keep in mind, this was 1980 and technology has
come a long way since then. Everyone I knew or had connections with,
people who work in certain places with lots of interesting things, all
said the same thing - we don't yet have the technology that can move
at those speeds and maneuver the ways those things did and do the
things those things did and all without sound. We had - and have -
interesting craft alright. But nothing like this."

Now, nearly 17 years after the event, Halt still doesn't know what he
and his men encountered. He remains as bewildered as the morning after
his fateful trip into Rendlesham Forest

Even with FOIA's release of the Halt Document in 1983, and with
considerable media attention that followed, no satisfactory
explanations have been presented as far as Halt is concerned.

The USAF, meanwhile, has offered no comment on the matter.

Now, in an effort to stop the rumors and objectively, rationally seek
the answer for once and for all what happened on those two nights just
outside of Bentwaters/Woodbridge complex, Penniston and Halt have come
forward to reveal the whole story. "Anywhere there's a vacuum
something moves it to fill it up," says Halt. "There's one individual*
who's traveled around, made the talk show circuit and claims to have
been present for the incidents. He's what I call a wannabe, someone
who maybe was hanging on the edge, picking up pieces of information.
He's claimed the wing commander went out and made contact with
holographic aliens and that we helped them repair their craft and all
kinds of things. None of which are true," says Halt.

*{This is clearly a reference to Larry Warren]

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

"Tt would be more interesting I suppose if there had been some alien
beings, but I never saw any," he says. "I never personally saw even

a definite shaped craft. I wish I had. Maybe I'd have a little better
feel for it. However, I certainly do believe the young airmen who were
out there the first night - especially Sgt. Penniston, who actually
touched the object. What we're trying to do now is set the facts
straight. We need to keep them straight or we’ll never get the
answers."

"I've listened intently to many explanations as to what we saw that
second night or what could have transpired, for the simple reason that
I kept reaching out for that one straw that would explain things,"
says Halt. "But nothing fits everything."

"There was a Russian satellite that I understand did a re-entry," he
continues. "I also understand that several of the large, bright
planets were aligned in an interesting way that night. Some have said
there was a meteor shower, and some have said that we mistook the
whole night's worth of activities for the rotating beacon on the
closest lighthouse, and the revolving blue light on top of a police
car - as ridiculous as that is. I actually consider the lighthouse as
a possible factor as we were in the woods that night, but it was not
the lighthouse. There's no doubt in my mind. The lighthouse was
clearly visible the whole time that night."

"None of those explanations explains an object that was moving
horizontally through the trees, that stopped and looked like it was
dripping something, all without sound, then 'explode' with barely a
sound and break into five separate objects that just disappear in the
sky," says Halt. "Lighthouses don't move horizontally through the
forest and around the trees. We never saw any civilians or local
police units. We never saw or heard any other activity other than the
anomalies - except the barnyard animals."

As for the indentations in the ground, Halt says, "Rabbit diggings?
In ground that was frozen, that wasn't even penetrated by our
vehicles? It doesn't make much sense to me."

And, how about mass hallucination? "It was not a hallucination," says
Halt.. "There were too many parties involved at too many different
locations. There was no missing time, time lapse, no hallucination,
no mass hypnosis."

The idea of psychological warfare doesn't jibe with Halt either. "The
idea that we or the British would be testing some kind of
psychological warfare just outside one of the most sensitive NATO
bases in Europe, where civilians could possibly be involved doesn't
make any sense either," he says. "We can do some very interesting
things alright, but some of the men in the weapons storage area saw
the objects in the sky as well as the object thdt projected the
pencil-like beam - and they were a mile and a half away, maybe two
from where we were, and I question whether or not they could have been
influenced by some kind of projection. I don't think so, not at that
distance."

In conclusion, Halt says: "Something unexplained happened. To this
day, I don't know what it was."

Southampton. Date
Section 40 3 November 1987

1. Thank you for your letter of 30 October in which you asked
how the Ministry of Defence deals with reports of “unexplained"
aerial sightings. This office is the focal point within the MOD
for correspondence of this nature. :

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
Significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "“UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if ~ resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

I. Thank you for your letter of 26 September and your fax of 2
October regarding a sighting over Pickering, North Yorkshire.

2. You asked whether there was any military aircraft operating
at high altitude in the area. Military aircraft wishing to operate
below 2000 feet in the UK Low Flying System are required to make a
booking covering those specific areas where low level activity is
to take place. As such, it is usual to be able to identify
military aircraft operating at low level without disproportionate
effort. However, because of its differing nature, the same booking
procedures do not apply to military aircraft activity above 2000
feet and we are unable to positively identify aircraft operating
in this heightband in the same way. Therefore I am, on this
occasion, unable to help you.

South Norwood, D/Sec(ASV64/3

London. Date

1. Thank you for your letter regarding “unidentified flying
objects".
2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines

any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. You asked for records relating to “UFOs". You will wish to be
aware that, as with other government departments, MOD files are
subject to the provisions of the Public Records Act of 1958 and
1967. This Act of Parliament states that official files generally
remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last
action has been taken, and at the thirty year point they are
transferred to the Public Record Office and into the public
domain. It was generally the case that before 1967 all "UFO"
report files were destroyed after five years because of limited
archive space and due to insufficient public interest in the
subject, there was no reason for them to be retained. Since 1967
due to an increase in public interest these files are now
routinely preserved. However, a few files from the Fifties and
Sixties did survive and may be viewed by members of the public at
the Public Record Office, Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey,
