1. Thank you for your letter of 22 September. This office is the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for reports of
"unexplained" aerial sightings.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/£lying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.

EXECUTIVE GRADE RECRUITMENT San enc. >,

Thank you for your letter of 5 September 1997 to requesting advice on
Careers within the Ministry of Defence. | have been as reply.

As with many Government Departments and Agencies the Ministry of Defence has
delegated most of it's personnel management functions, including recruitment. This
means that we no longer recruit nationwide for executive grades and now mainly cover
the Greater London area only. Recruitment to posts in other geographical locations and
certain specialist functions is carried out by the authority responsible for that particular
area. For ease, | attach a list of the major recruitment branches. You might wish to
contact some of these or your local MOD establishment for further details.

At present there are no plans to recruit executive grades in the London area, although
there will shortly be a campaign for Administrative Assistants.

Unfortunately we have no up to date literature covering our limited area within the MOD
and there is, at present, no general MOD brochure. However, for your reference, |
enclose a copy of the most recent literature covering the work of executive grades in the
MOD. Although this is somewhat out of date it should give you an idea of what you could
expect should you choose to work for the Ministry.

| am sorry to provide what appears to be a Slightly negative reply to your enquiry, but |
hope that it is of some use to your future career planning. | would like to take this
opportunity to thank you for the interest you have shown in the Ministry of Defence.

incerely,

1. Thank you for your letters of 5 and 28 September in which you
described an unidentified aircraft observed by your neighbours in
early April. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence of this nature.

2. Without a date it is not possible to say whether there was
any military aircraft activity in the Birmingham area although, in
principle, the whole of the UK is open to low flying training by
military aircraft. There are certain locations which are excluded
such as major built-up areas and restricted airspace around civil
airfields, but outside such exclusion zones less populated areas
will see training flights from time to time. However, as
Birmingham is a large city with its own airport, it is unlikely
that the aircraft your neighbours saw was military. I would advise
you to check with the Civil Aviation Authority at CAA House, 45-59
Kingsway, London, WC2B 6TE to find out whether any civil aircraft
were operating in the area.

3. I have looked back through our sighting report files and have
found that the MOD did not receive any reports of unusual aerial
activity in the first half of April from the Birmingham area. We
are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest
that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised
foreign military aircraft which is the MOD's only concern.

1. Thank you for your letter of 9 August addressed to the Prime
Minister. Your letter has been passed to the Ministry of Defence
and this office is the focal point within the MOD for
correspondence of this nature. I have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no “UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

Yours gi

MINISTERS ' CORRESPONDENCE UNIT
001703

To Set. OWS) 2 Ref No [1997

Date

The attached letter(s) which the Prime Minister has received has been
forwarded to this Department for official action. No.10's letter codes are as

follows:
(a) - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
send a full reply within 20 working days.

B - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
consider whether there is anything which can usefully
be said to the correspondent and action accordingly.

Cc - No acknowledgement has been sent. In this case,

however, it is obviously important that both an
acknowledgement and a full reply are sent.

Unless specifically asked to do so, there is no need for you to copy your
replies to this office.

A new Open Government Code of Practice came into force in January 1997.
All replies to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in

cr o ~ I further information is available from DOMD on extension

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep record of
their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the
accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be performed
throughout the year.

Your reference

Our reference

: D/Sec(AS)/64/3
Milton Keynes, Date

ection 40 fll |} October 1997

1. Thank you for your letter of 27 June and 21 August addressed
to the Prime Minister regarding "UFOs". Your letters have been
passed to the Ministry of Defence and this office is the focal
point within the MOD for correspondence of this nature. I am sorry
for the delay in responding.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
Significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity. Unless there is evidence
of a potential military threat, and to date no "UFO" report has
revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise
nature of each sighting reported to us.

3. You have asked that all existing files relating to "UFO"
Sightings be made available to the public. You will wish to be
aware that, as with other government departments, MOD files are
subject to the provisions of the Public Records Act of 1958 and
1967. This Act of Parliament states that official files generally
remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last
action has been taken, and at the thirty year point they are
transferred to the Public Record Office and into the public
domain. It was generally the case that before 1967 all "UFO"
report files were destroyed after five years because of limited
archive space and due to insufficient public interest in the
subject, there was no reason for them to be retained. Since 1967
due to an increase in public interest these files are now
routinely preserved. However, a few files from the Fifties and
Sixties did survive and may be viewed by members of the public at
the Public Record Office, Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey,
TW9 4DU. The references of these files are as follows:
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

AIR 16/1199
AIR 20/7390 AIR 2/16918
AIR 20/9320 AIR 2/17318

AIR 20/9321 AIR 2/17526
AIR 20/9322 AIR 2/17527
AIR 20/9994 AIR 2/17982
PREM 11/855 AIR 2/17983
4. With regard to the alleged events at Rendlesham Forest in

December 1980, as there was no evidence to substantiate an event
of defence concern no further investigation into the matter was
necessary. Although a number of allegations have subsequently been
made about these reported events, nothing has emerged over the
last 16% years which has given us reason to believe that the
original assessment made by this Department was incorrect.

5. The "UFO" sightings in Belgium of 30/31 March 1990 occurred
in the central part of the country and the Belgian authorities
have confirmed that there was no evidence of a threat associated
with what was seen. No detections were made on UK air defence
systems which would have been completely understandable and
acceptable since the sightings occurred outside the UK Air Defence
Region. We are, therefore, entirely satisfied that, with no
unauthorized incursions of the UK Air Defence Region, there was no
threat to the UK. The issue remains a matter for the Belgian

authorities.

their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be performed

Dear Prime Minister,

Should this letter reach your desk, let me first ask that you read it with an open mind
and weigh up what is a considerable body of evidence for some very strange
occurrences. | am referring to the subject of UFO's, a field of inquiry I have followed
for a good many years, and to which I given a great deal of thought.

I should also like to state here that | am not a conspiracy theorist, nor a doomsday
scenarist preaching the danger of UFO invasions, and believe that without doubt,
most UFO sightings can be accounted for by mistaken identity, natural phenomena, or
outright fakery. However, there remains a convincing body of evidence to suggest the
existence of real nuts and bolts machines of unusual and radically advanced design,
and certainly I feel that the attitude and behaviour of past British governments has
done a lot to convince people that the truth 1s being obscured.

A number of possibilities present themselves.

1) UFO's do not exist.
2) UFO's exist but the government has no knowledge of their nature or purpose.
3) UFO's exist and the government is covering up the situation.

The first possibility simply cannot be supported by the overwhelming evidence to the
contrary. The second is the most likely, but then you have to ask if the avowed
disinterest by British governments is either stupidity or negligence. Of course a lot of
people would like to believe the third option, though frankly I think that successive
British governments are more likely to have been kept in ignorance by the Americans,
who undoubtedly know more than they are telling. The C.LA spent years denying
they had documents related to UFO's. Through the Freedom Of Information Act, we
now know there are tens of thousands, and that in the late 1940's a US Airforce

Page 1 of 2

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

general writing in an official inter-departmental memo was convinced that UFO's
were real, though an extraterrestrial hypothesis was not officially presented.

I am writing to urge you to open up any and all files that exist on this subject, and
would further urge you not to take the word of your military. Appoint independent
investigators and have the records searched. Not all UFO sightings can be easily
explained away; there are convincing cases, and even RAF pilots have encountered
UFO's, several of whom have gone on the record to say so. What ever happened to the
reports they filed and what was the official reaction and response in Whitehall? Even
if these are bizarre natural phenomena, they need investigating, and I think the British
public deserve to know what it is they are seeing in our skies. Following please find
enclosed two case reports, which I think are representative of the scope, nature and
seriousness of the phenomena.

I will be sending a copy of this letter to my local MP, and will request that he raises
this matter in Parliament, and that specifically he obtains a statement on the events at
Rendlesham forest, (see attachment) with a view to requesting a full parliamentary
inquiry and report.

Yours sincerely,

Page 2 of 2
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

UFO Briefing Document: 2 Cases Of Defence Significance.

Rendlesham Forest, December 27th/28th 1980.

Without doubt the most amazing UFO event in the UK, with full documentary
evidence available and witnesses including the deputy base commander of a
American nuclear armed air base. On the night of December 27th, perimeter guards
at Woodbridge, a joint British/American base, spotted lights in nearby trees of
Rendlesham forest. Believing a plane had crashed, they set out to investigate and
were confronted with an glowing triangular shaped object of metallic appearance,
which then departed at speed. Physical trace evidence in the form of indentations in
the ground were found the next day matching the configuration of the object, as were
abnormal radiation readings. The next night, deputy base commander Lt col. Charles
Halt was called out to the same woods, and witnessed an extraordinary display by
objects manoeuvring in the forest and the skies above. All the above is in Halt's
report, a report which could only be obtained THREE years later by investigators in
the USA through the Freedom Of Information Act. Curiously, the MOD denied the
event for years, even though a copy of Halt's report had been sent to them on January
13th 1981. I believe you will find that the issue was raised in Parliament in 1996, but
Nicholas Soames (standing in for Defence Secretary Michael Portillo) stated that the
document was judged to contain “nothing of defence significance’. This is
preposterous. If one of the guards (who has gone public) can be believed,
Woodbridge was the secret repository of a massive nuclear stockpile, but even so, to
have the deputy commander of an important base and his men hallucinating or
fabricating such a story should be of serious concern to anyone with a degree of
common sense. To actually have unknown aerial vehicles in the vicinity would surely
then be of paramount interest to the authorities as a serious security violation. I
believe you may have received a copy of a book on this subject "Left At East Gate",
by one of the guards that night, though I am open minded enough to recommend that
you take some of the contents with a pinch of salt. The events he describes may be a
little colourful compared to the reality, but undoubtedly something extremely odd
happened at Rendlesham that cannot be easily dismissed.

Belgium jet fighter intercept attempts.

In 1990 over 2 consecutive nights (30th-31st March), Belgium police and numerous
members of the public reported mysterious objects flying overhead, often described
as large black triangles. The objects were tracked on radar and Belgium Airforce
fighters were scrambled, but every time they achieved a lock with their internal radar,
the objects performed outrageous manoeuvres and escaped at high speed. A general
of the Belgium airforce subsequently went on television and showed recordings of the
fighter aircraft's on-board radar tapes which backed up the story. Of course these may
have been American stealth fighters, but this raises two questions.

1) Were the American covertly flying stealth fighters over the airspace of an ally in
order to judge the effectiveness of their technology, a serious breach of trust and a

Page | of 2
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

considerable danger to the pilots of the intercepting aircraft and civilians on the
ground?

2) Since the objects performed manoeuvres beyond the scope of our present
technology, how is it the Americans have made such an advance?

As has been pointed out in a book on these events, the reporting system NATO
countries employ would demand that the UK air defences be informed of these
events, yet the previous government insists that no such communication was received.

Furthermore, if there is no interest by American government (as they loudly protest)
in UFO's, why does a recently declassified distribution list headed "Belgium and the
UFO issue" have a distribution list over 40 lines long, disseminating copies to the
White House, the Joint Chiefs Of Staff, and numerous other major intelligence and
military offices.

A similar document on a Chinese UFO conference (unrelated to the Belgium events)

includes the Defence Intelligence Service at the MOD on its distribution list. Why
would they be interested in what the Chinese thought of UFO's?

1. Thank you for your letter of 1 September regarding the
alleged RAF Bentwaters/Rendlesham Forest incident.

2. You have stated that you wish to visit the site and would
like confirmation that it is permitted to do so. RAF Bentwaters
was sold to Bentwaters Investments Limited on 15 May this year
therefore it is no longer MOD property.

3. The memorandum written by Lt Col Halt, Deputy Base Commander
at the time of the alleged incident, was assessed by officials in
the Department responsible for air defence matters at the time.
Since the judgement was that it contained nothing of defence
significance no further action was taken. The memo has been in the
public domain for a number of years.

4. Finally, I would like to assure you that the Royal Air Force
is responsible for ensuring that the integrity of the United
Kingdom Air Defence Region is maintained. It is the role of the
RAF to ensure that no hostile or unauthorized aircraft enter UK
airspace. As such the UK Air Defence Region is continually
policed to ensure that no craft which might represent a threat to
the security of this country enters our airspace. To date it
remains the case that the MOD is not aware of any evidence which
might substantiate the existence of extraterrestrial phenomena,
and no threat to the UK has been discerned which has been
attributed to a "UFO".

1. Thank you for your letter of 3 September regarding an
unexplained aerial sighting observed on 16 August 1997 over Fife.
As you will by now be aware, the letter you sent to RAF Leuchars
has been forwarded to this office for action; please accept my
letter as a reply to both.

2. I have looked back through our sighting report files and have
found that the MOD did not receive any reports from Scotland for
16 August 1997.

3. You asked whether any commercial or military aircraft had
been operating over the Fife area on 16 August. In principle, the
whole of the UK is open to low flying training by military
aircraft. There are certain locations which are excluded such as
major built-up areas and restricted airspace around civil
airfields, but outside such exclusion zones less populated areas
will see a proportion of training flights from time to time.
Therefore, it is possible that military aircraft could have been
in the region. I would advise you to check with the Civil Aviation
Authority at CAA House, 45-59 Kingsway, London, WC2B 6TE to find
out whether any civil aircraft were operating in the area. In
addition, there are many microlight/hang-glider flying sites and
private helicopter landing sites around Fife.

4. I have returned your SAE as we have our own postal

arrangements.

1. Thank you for your letter of 12 August concerning RAF Rudloe

Weston Super Mare, Date

Section 40, ss (A Cctober 1997

. Thank you for your letter of 17 September in which you have
joomemare information on "UFOs" for your English essay. This
office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence relating to unexplained aerial sightings.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
Significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain

FBID [Foreign Broadcasts Information Division] has one broad-

On 2 December 1952 H. Marshall Chadwell, Assistant Director of
Scientific Intelligence, sent a Secret memorandum to the CIA Director,
discussing the preparation of the National Security Council directive
referred to earlier. Paragraph 4 makes particularly interesting reading:

Recent reports reaching CLA indicated that further action was
desirable and another briefing by the cognizant A-2 [Air Force
Intelligence] and ATIC [Air Technical Intelligence Center]
personnel was held on 25 November. At this time, the reports of
incidents convince us that there is something going on that must
have immediate attention . . . Sightings of unexplained objects at
great altitudes and traveling at high speeds in the vicinity of major
USS. defense installations are of such nature that they are not
attributable to natural phenomena or known types of aerial

vehicles.

The Robertson Panel Report

Evidently, by the end of 1952 the situation became so worrying for the CIA
that a panel of scientists was convened by the Office of Scientific
Intelligence, and secret meetings were held at the Pentagon from 14 to 17
January 1953. Although sanitized copies had been available to certain
officials outside the CIA for a number of years, the Robertson Panel
Report (sometimes referred to as the Durant Report) was not completely
declassified until 1975. To this day there are those who believe that the
report has not been released in its entirety. I wrote to the CIA in 1975
requesting a copy (under the FOIA), and it arrived a few months later.
Members of the Scientific Advisory Panel were Dr H. P. Robertson
(Chairman), whose speciality was physics and weapons systems; Dr Luis
Alvarez (physics and radar); Dr Lloyd V. Berkner (geophysics); Dr Samuel
Goudsmit (atomic structure and statistical problems), and Dr Thornton
Page (astronomy and astrophysics). The associate members were Dr J.
Allen Hynek (astronomy) and Frederick C. Durant (missiles and rockets)
Interviewees were Brigadier William H. Garland, Commandin;
General of Air Technical Intelligence Centers Dr H. Marshall Chadwell
Assistant Director of the CIA/OSI; Ralph L. Clark, Deputy Assistan
Director CLA/OSI; Lieutenant Colonel F. C. Oder and D. B. Stevenson
OSI staff members; Philip G. Strong, Chief, Operations Staff, OSI; Stephe!
T. Possony, Acting Chief, Special Study Group, Directorate of Air Forc
Intelligence; Colonels William A. Adams and Wesley S. Smith, also of Ai

These significant comments were made to the Latvian-born American
journalist Henry Gris during an interview in 1981. Zigel further claimed
that he had 50,000 UFO reports on file in the computer of the Moscow
Aviation Institute, adding that from materials in his own archives he had

Zigel went on to claim that at least seven landings of extraterrestrial
spacecraft had occurred in the vicinity of Moscow between June 1977 and
September 1979. He believed that there are three basic categories of UFO
occupant: spacemen, the least frequently observed, who are very tall beings,
3 metres or so in height; humanoids, who are in general so similar to us in
height and in many other respects that they could most probably mingle
here undetected; and what Zigel called aliens, who are around 1 metre in
height and, although resembling us in some respects, possess relatively
large heads with no trace of hair, protruding eyes set far apart, wrinkled
faces, and a pair of large nostrils by way of a nose.

In addition to these categories of what he termed ‘flesh-and-blood
extraterrestrials’ Zigel stated that the spacecraft carry crews of robots or
androids which possess the ability to disappear and reappear at will and,
not being subject to the physical laws of our planet, seem to be
‘deliberately constructed in order to confound all our notions of space,
matter, time and dimensions’.“”

My own findings, from over thirty-five years’ research worldwide,

correspond with those of Zigel.

Dramatic Events at Baikonur

. ae to Henry Gris, scientists Dr Alexei Zolotov and Dr Vladimir
+ Azhazha told him that two UFOs hovered over the Baikonur Space Centre,
Kazakhstan, for fourteen seconds on 1 June 1982, one directly above
launch pad no. 1. The following day bolts and rivets were found which

The Soviet Union 247

allegedly had been ‘sucked out’ of the support towers, causing welded \,

sections to come apart. The other UFO reportedly hovered over a housing
complex, knocking out thousands of panes of glass, or making fine holes
in them. As a consequence, the entire cosmodrome was said to have been
put out of action for at least two weeks.

This story was confirmed to Gordon Creighton by Henry Gris, who
added that even UFO sceptic James Oberg of NASA had admitted to him
that the base had indeed been out of commission during the period in
question, and apparently could find no explanation for it.“

Commission for the Investigation of Anomalous Atmospheric Phenomena

By the beginning of 1983 the Russians were once again admitting that

UFOs were a serious subject. An article in Sovietskaya Kultura of 6 f

January stated that the existence of UFOs should not be ruled out, and’
revealed that a Soviet Air Force pilot had had an encounter with one in
1981. The article said that there were still many unexplained phenomena
behind the reports, and urged scientists to collate as much information as
possible.*”

In February 1984 the Commission for the Investigation of Anomalous
Atmospheric Phenomena was established in Moscow, although its official
announcement in the West was delayed until May. Affiliated to the
Committee for the Protection of Natural Environment of the All-Union
Council of Scientific Technical Societies, the new commission was headed
by the distinguished former cosmonaut Pavel Popovich. Popovich told the
trade-union newspaper Trud (Labour) that there had been hundreds of
UEO reports each year in the Soviet Union, and that most could be
explained away. But scientists had been disturbed by events in Gorky, 250
miles from Moscow, the previous year, which defied rational analysis.

On the evening of 27 March 1983 air traffic controllers at Gorky
Airport had observed a steel-grey cigar-shaped object flying towards them
which failed to respond to radio contact. It was about the size of a
conventional aircraft but lacked wings, tail or fin, and was flying at an
altitude of 3,000 feet at a speed of up to 125 m.p-h. The object behaved
erratically, flying 45 miles to the south-east of Gorky before turning to
head back to the airport, finally vanishing 25 miles to the north of the city.

Popovich added that the new commission was taking this report very
seriously, since the sighting had been made by reliable and well-trained
aviation experts who had given precise and scientific observations, and
who had tracked the UFO on radar for forty minutes.

The Masters of Silence

Fernand Lagarde, one of France’s most effective researchers, also expressed
serious misgivings about the state of official research, believing that the
‘Open-door policy’ seemingly initiated with the establishment of GEPAN
had come to an end. Lagarde found that his requests for information and
documents from official sources were blocked at every stage, just as
elsewhere in the world. ‘We have now to face the fact that alid. . . marked
secret, has come down on all official research,’ he wrote in 198]. ‘Sighting
reports likely to be of interest to us no longer find their way to us.’ The
“Masters of Silence’, as he called them, had taken over once more,'°
Another distinguished French researcher who shares this view is the

astrophysicist Dr Pierre Guérin of the French Institute of Astrophysics and
a senior research officer in the CNRS. In 1984 I met Dr Guérin in Paris,

and over lunch we discussed the cover-up. The demise — or apparent
demise - of GEPAN was first on the agenda. ‘It’s now limited to only two
people,’ said Dr Guérin, ‘Monsieur Velasco, the head — he’s not even a
Scientist, he’s an engineer — and his secretary. That is all!’ GEPAN, he
confirmed, was under the aegis of the CNES, which itself was under the
direction of a scientific committee which was not well-disposed towards
the subject.

current scientific framework:

A Landing Case investigated by Intelligence Specialists

The following year another disturbing encounter, reported by a reservist
with the Cuban Army, led Fidel Castro to ask Soviet intelligence specialists
to conduct extensive investigations. Reported in detail by Jacques Vallée in
his book UFO Chronicles of the Soviet Union, the following is a synopsis of
this extraordinary case.

Shortly after midnight on 14 June 1968, several bursts of machine-gun
fire were heard coming from a location in the vicinity of Cabafias, where
Isidro Puentes Ventura was on guard duty. At dawn, Puentes was found
unconscious by an Army patrol and taken to a hospital in Pinar del Rio,
where he remained in shock and unable to speak for six days. Removed to
the neurological ward at the Naval Hospital in Havana, Puentes as
diagnosed to be suffering from emotional trauma, remaining in shock for;
another week. /

At the site where Puentes had been posted, Cuban and Soviet
intelligence investigators found forty-eight spent cartridges and four-
teen bullets apparently flattened by impact, as well as equally spaced
indentations in the ground, indicating that a heavy device had landed.
Tests revealed that the soil had been exposed to a high degree of
heat.

When Puentes recovered consciousness, he reported that he had come
to within 150 feet of a brilliant round object on the ground, with a dome

and several ‘antennas’ on top. Convinced that the device was an American “

helicopter, Puentes fired about forty rounds at it. The craft turned orange
.and emitted a strong whistling sound — Puentes’ last recollection before
losing consciousness.

Soviet intelligence specialists subjected Puentes to a fifty-hour
interrogation, after which he was examined by a team of psychiatrists and
put through fifteen hypnosis sessions. As Vallée reports, no contradictions
were found in his story.”°

South Africa

Like other air forces around the world, the South African Air Force takes
UFOs seriously. In 1953 a Defence Headquarters spokesman in Pretoria
revealed that there had been some reliable sightings by SAAF officers, and
added: “There is now a regular exchange of information between our Air
Force and the Royal Air Force. Reports have also been referred to military
intelligence.”° In 1955 the Air Chief of Staff admitted that the South

illusion, no deception, no imagination.’

Tayside. Date

1. I am writing with reference to your recent report of an
unexplained aerial sighting which you observed on 5 October 1997.
The details of your report have been passed from RAF Leuchars to
this office as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence relating to "unidentified flying
objects".

2. First perhaps it would be useful if I were to explain the
MOD's interest with respect to reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects". The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of "UFOs"

it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence
that the UK Air Defence Region might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 5 October
in Scotland, and we are satisfied that there is no corroborating
evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was
breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

Ministry of Defence for correspondence of this nature. I have been
asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial Jlifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.
Yous simoyaly,

1. Thank you for your letter of 12 September to

and your letters of 27 July and 12 September to the Prime
Minister. All three letters concerned the alleged incident at
Rendlesham Forest in 1980. I have been asked to reply.

2. In Section 40) letter to you dated 28 August, the

Ministry of Defence's position regarding the Rendlesham Forest
incident was explained, as was the MOD's general stance on “UFO"
matters. However, I feel it is worth reiterating that the Royal
Air Force is responsible for ensuring that the integrity of the
United Kingdom Air Defence Region is maintained and it is the role
of the RAF to ensure that no hostile or unauthorized aircraft
enter UK airspace. As such, the UK Air Defence Region is
continually policed to ensure that no aircraft which might
represent a threat to the security of this country enters our
airspace.

3. In the context of our responsibilities for ensuring the
effective defences of the United Kingdom, the MOD and HM Forces
remain vigilant for any signs of activity which might be of
defence significance, from whatever source. This is why the MOD
has a focal/co-ordination point which looks at reports of
“unexplained" aerial sightings. However, if no threat is
discerned, as has been the case in all reported "UFO" sightings to
date, we do not seek to determine the precise nature of every
seemingly inexplicable sight in the sky. In line with our defence
responsibilities we are neither under remit, nor are we resourced,
to undertake such tasks.

4. The MOD does not study or research “UFOs" and this office,

which forms part of an RAF Secretariat Branch, is not constituted
as a "UFO" information bureau.

Yours, Sivawoly,

ection 40. g October 1997

1. I am writing with reference to your message left on the
Secretariat (Air Staff) answerphone in which you asked for
information on “UFOs" for your school project. This office is the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence
relating to "UFOs".

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

You's Sucoray, _

The Missing File

The file which contained so many revelations about departments in the MoD investigating UFOs went

missing after Nick Redfern was filmed for a dectuumentary called Out Of This World, around March 1995.
The official explanation for the missing file was that jt has been ‘misfiled’.

We suspected otherwise, but if took the intervention of MP leuan Wyn Jones, who asked a question
in the House Of Commons. to bring the file back. The file was’ returned two days after the question
being tabled, ‘but still tne excuse given was that it had been misfiled - this was after one year of looking
for it and one major file search (known as a census).

In a visit to the Public Records Office in 1996, myself, Richard Conway and Chris Fowler were told by
Vivian Bales that the file was probably with the MoD as they often recalled sensitive files which they
may have released information in by mistake. It was her opinion that this is possibly what had

happefled. We did not prompt her for this explahation and she gave it to us-as an unofficial comment,
I didn’t buy the official missing file story so went on my own search for who might have had the file

at the MoD. | used contacts | have in government to point me toward the relevant MoD department,
where | might find an answer | was given the details of a small department which is housed inside

the Metropole Building in Northumberland Avenue. After speaking to the staff there | was put onto”
the Deputy Departmental Records Officer, Mr | D Goode.

Official Clamp-Down

The Central Records Management checked their records and confirmed that records the file being

inside the MoD in November 1996. This proved that an MP and members of the public were being
lied to about the whereabouts af this file. Both the MeD and Public Records Office had assured us
that they bad carried out proper checks on the wherealkouts of the file. However, if they had then
surely they would have discovered taat.it was booked out to the MoD?

The final part of the saga is that an offi clamp-down has come into force. We were toid by

the MoD that Central Records Management had made a clerical error, and that the records should
have read November 1995

One thing is now clear we have the ammunition to fight the government over the files of these
seven secret departments who files do not appear inthe Public Records Office. It has even been
hinted to.me by one insider that if any files for these departments did exist, then they would not

become public because most of these files are Defence Intelligence orientated. These types of
files rarely get released, if af all!

For Your Information

Space restrictions mean that we cannot include ail the papers which make up the MoD file

bundle, but if anyone requires the full documents they are available for public viewing in
Truthseekers Magazine.

For a copy, please send a cheque payable to Truthseekers. Magazine for £2.00, and you will
be sent a photocopied bundle.

1. Thank you for your letters of 14 August and 2 September in
which you have given details of several unusual aerial sightings
seen above East London throughout the summer. JI would like to
apologise for the delay in replying.

2. In your letter of 14 August, you stated that you had seen
various "unexplained" aircraft in the sky above your local park. I
have looked back through our sighting report files and have found
that the MOD did not receive any other "UFO" reports from the East
London area on the dates listed.

3. As the sightings were all in the vicinity of the London City
Airport, you asked if air traffic control could check if any
strange objects had appeared on their radar. I would suggest that
you either get in touch with the airport itself or write to the
Civil Aviation Authority at CAA House, 45-59 Kingsway, London,
WC2B 6TE. It is highly unlikely that any military aircraft would
have been operating in the East London region as major built-up
areas are generally avoided by military aircraft.

4. I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's
airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous policing of
the UK Air Defence Region by the Royal Air Force and we remain
vigilant for any potential military threat. Our enquiries have
revealed that on 2 September 1997 there was no unusual air defence
activity, no penetration by hostile military aircraft of the UK
Air Defence Region, no unknown aircraft activity on any military
radars and that no Quick Reaction Alert aircraft were scrambled.

5. Finally, please find enclosed your video which I am returning

with this letter.
Yous SMR,

nature, they are totally unlike any known (to me) aircraft and the fact that they were
hovering in formation seems to discount the possibility of them being balloons of any
kind. To my eyes, they seem to be under intelligent control which I think begs the
question of who are flying these objects and, more importantly, is the MoD aware that
these UFO's are invading British airspace time after time? I feel strongly that immediate
steps should be implemented to ascertain whether these objects are authorised to be
flying in crowded British airspace in the immediate vicinity of City Airport and, of
more concern, whether they are of any defence significance.

intend to make this video tape available to the press and media in the hope of
uncovering some answers and welcome your views on this urgent matter.

Yours Faithfully,

on, Date
Stoke on Trent, 6 October 1997
Staffordshire.
1. I am writing with reference to your report of an unexplained

aerial sighting which you observed on 21 September 1997. The
details of your report have been passed from the: Staffordshire
Police to the Ministry of Defence and this office is the focal
point for correspondence relating to “unidentified flying
objects".

2. First perhaps it would be useful if I were to explain the
MOD's interest with respect to reports of "unidentified flying
objects". The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of "UFOs"
it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence
that the UK Air Defence Region might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of “UFO" sightings for 21
September in the Staffordshire area, and we are satisfied that

there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

1. Thank you for your letter of 27 August 1997 in which you
described an unexplained aerial sighting observed in your area
over the past few weeks. This office is the focal point within the
Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to queries of this
nature and I have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was ‘seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain

1. Thank you for your letter of 18 August 1997 in which you
described an unexplained aerial sighting seen on the evening of 9

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 9 August
in or around Cheshire, and we are satisfied that there is no
corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft which, as
stated above, is our only interest.

ythenshawe,

18" August, 1997

I’m writing to you, so as to make a formal report of the
observation myself and my girlfriend made of a strange light in the sky over
Wilmslow, Cheshire at 22.25hrs on the night of Aug, 9" 97 from the rear balcony
of the Samuel Finny public house.

I must first of all explain that I refer to our observation as a light
and not an aircraft because at no time during our observation did we see any
structured shape. The light was very bright, brilliant white and sharp, it was
bigger and brighter than any star, about the size of the two most brilliant stars in
the sky put together. At no time could we see any beam emanating from the
light source, the kind you would expect to see if the light was a search light of
some sort. The light moved from our right to our left in a straight line and it did
not appear to change it’s altitude at any time. The speed was much faster than
you would expect from say the police helicopter but slower than a low level
fighter jet that I sometimes see flying over my place of work. At no time what so
ever did we here any sound emanating from the light. After about 8 - 10 seconds
of our observation I realised that the light was dimming at a rapid rate, within 3
seconds it had diminished to a reddish dot and then was gone, We did not see
any Anti-collision lights throughout our observation, either as we were observing
the light or after it had gone. The light disappeared well within our range of
view and didn’t go behind any building, tree top or cloud.

© After later studying a map I could ascertain that the light was
travelling in the direction of the British Aerospace Aerodrome at Woodford.
Totally without any doubt in our minds, what we saw was neither a Star, Comet,
Satellite or Meteorite. 1 can tell you that it definitely was flying and not falling or
being blown by the wind. It possessed no physical shape I could ascertain and
was not displaying any of the normal warning lights you associate with aircraft
flying at night.

“TD reported our observation to the Wilmslow Police and to the
Tower at Manchester Airport the next evening. I must say that I was very
surprised and upset by the response I got from the Tower as they were most
obstructive and unwilling to take our observation of something flyinignf ch: ~

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

the Wilmslow sky, only a few miles ( a minute or two, flying time ) from the
Airport, at night with no lights on, as serious enough to log my report and not
try to insult my intelligence by claiming it was a star. If the man I was speaking
to at the Tower, who incidentally would not give me his name is typical of the
people we entrust with the safety of the sky’s, then I’m afraid a disaster is
imminent.

1 would like to here from you if you have any information that may
solve the identification mystery of our observation. Also if you could let me
know if any procedure exists for the Tower to log my report with the M.O.D as I
believe it could be classed (and was referred to by the Tower) as a U.F.O.

Awaiting your responce,

As an investigator for BUFORA in the Durham/North Yorks area, I have been
approached by several members of the public and advised of some unusual
aerial activity on Thursday 14th August last, overhead Pickering, Nor¥&
Yorks.

At approx 2300 hrs local, a group of people witnessed what they initially
thought was the Mir station passing above them in a NE direction, however,
these people then saw an object of the same density and altitude moving in
the opposite SW direction. These objects passed each other in close
proximity then came to a stop. At this time, two other objects, one from
the east, the other from the west, approached the two origional objects and
also came to a stop. These people observed these objects for about a minute
before the objects dispersed in varying directions.

I have since been in contact with Newcastle Airport, Teeside Airport, and
RAF Leeming in an effort to confirm air traffic at that time. Each agency
have confirmed that no air traffic was in that area at that time.

Because of the seemingly high altitude of these objects, I also contacted
RAF Fylingdale for a possible explaination, they too had no record or
knowledge of the incident.
I have since contacted LATCC, Aeronautical Infirmation Service (Military)
who would not confirm or deny of any activity on the date, time and area as
mentioned above. LATCC referred me to your department.
I would be grateful if you could confirm any military activity which would
clarify this sighting. Also, can you advise me if any other agency or

Warwickshire. |X september 1997
1. Thank you for your letter of 7 September 1997 in which you

requested any information the Ministry of Defence may hold on two
unexplained sightings.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. I have looked back through our sighting report files and have
found that the MOD did not receive any reports for 4 November 1996
or 5 August 1997 in or around the areas specified. I have returned
your SAE as we have our own postal arrangements.

Yours sincaratyy,

Dear ALIEN ENCOUNTERS,
Please find enclosed a true
story for possible inclusion in
your magazine. After reading
the other paranormal/UFO
magazines, I have come to
the conclusion that ALIEN
ENCOUNTERS is by far the most
informative and interesting
read — it leaves the other
mags standing!

The evening of 4th
November 1996 turned out
to be a very strange one
indeed. It was a Monday, and
this meant I had the pleasure
of taking out the stinking
rubbish bags for the
dustmen. It was about 10pm,
and as I walked back up the
driveway after dumping the
rubbish bags I noticed a
number of bright white lights
flickering through the clouds
to the north-west.

It was very difficult to tell
how many lights there were
as they were moving about
so quickly, but at a guess I'd
say there were about 20 or
so. At first I thought it could
be lightning, but there wasn’t
any forecast and the lights
were darting about the sky
very rapidly, staying in an
area about the size of the
Plough. Their movements
were very much like that of a
swarm of gnats on a hot
summer’s day.

They appeared to be
moving eastwards and after
observing them for about five
minutes I rushed into the
house to tell my mum. She
reluctantly dragged herself to
the kitchen door to take a
look, after much persuasion.
My mum is very narrow-
minded when it comes to the
subject of UFOs, but even she
was amazed by what she
saw. She seemed quite
frightened and went back
indoors after a few minutes.

I knew I could get a better
view if [ went up to Dover’s
Hill, which is half a mile

48

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Asove This phato is taken from Campden Hole, which turned out to be an ideal skywatching site for Mark Nobes on 4th November

1996. Whilst there he saw a group of white and ble lights perform strange manoeuvres in the sky.

away, and decided to drive up
there. Unfortunately it was
facing slightly too far east, so
I drove to what is locally
known as ‘Campden Hole’.
This turned out to be ideal as
it’s very high up and away
from any street lights. It
points towards Broadway
and Cheltenham.

After about ten minutes of
viewing the lights, half of
them turned blue and they all
came together to form a sort
of oval shape. Then a larger
blue light appeared in the
centre of them — 1 wasn’t sure
where it came from ~ and
they all moved very rapidly
eastwards and disappeared in
a matter of seconds. It was a
great shame that the sky was
overcast as I’m sure that the
view would have been even
more fascinating had the sky
been clear. I felt sure that it
wasn’t a laser show as the
only shows are in August at
Long Marston airfield when
the Phoenix festival and
Bulldog Motorcycle Bash are
on. Besides, these lights were
too far west and didn’t form
a regular pattern like laser
lights usually do.

Alien Gardening

The next morning, I
witnessed something even
stranger. | went outside with
the dog at about 8.10pm and
noticed that all of the daffodil
bulbs, which were recently
planted, were lying on top of
the ground. Not a single bulb
out of the 200 which we
planted remained under the
soil. | thought that maybe a
mole or even the birds had
pulled them out, but surely
not every single bulb? I am
still trying to find an
explanation to this mystery
and whether it had any
connection with the strange
lights I saw.

This is only the second UFO
encounter I’ve had and I hope
it isn’t the last as I found the
whole event pretty
fascinating. I've been quite
keen on astronomy ever since
I was a young lad and this
has to be the most exciting
event I’ve witnessed. Much
more interesting than Hale-
Bopp! 1 wonder if any other
readers have experienced any
similar encounters or if
anyone else in the North
Cotswolds saw anything on

the same night? I have
enclosed a photo taken from
‘Campden Hole’, which is a
country lane between
Chipping Campden and
Broadway and situated next
to Fish Hill. This is where I
viewed the UFOs.

Please keep up the first class
work and may I wish you
every success for the future.
Mark Nobes,

Chipping Campden.

ton 4a. 1k September 1997

Dasy

1. Thank you for your recent letter regarding "unidentified
flying objects". This office is the focal point within the
Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to this subject.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“"UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

Yours sin

I am writing to you because I am doing a
project on U.F.O's and I was wondering if you
would send me some information to help me. If
you would could you send me a hand written letter
on your views: are there U.F.O's? I would also like
some leaflets and posters if possible.

Thank-you very much.

1. Thank you for your report of an unexplained sighting which

ou observed from your home on the evening of 5 September 1997. - EES <0
Section fa longer works in this department and I have been asked

respond to your report.

2a First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to’provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 5
September in or around London, and we are satisfied that there is
no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

1. I am writing with reference to your message left on the

Secretariat (Air Staff) answerphone in which you reported an
"unidentified flying object" seen on 14, 15 and 16 September 1997.
This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence of this nature.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights’ or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4, With regard to your particular observations, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 14, 15 or
16 September in or around Kent, and we are satisfied that there is
no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

Edinburgh.

»

1. Thank you for your letter of 14 July addressed to the Prime
Minister regarding the "UFO" phenomenon. Your letter has been
passed to the Ministry of Defence and this office is the focal
point within the MOD for correspondence of this nature. I have
been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

5. You asked if "UFO" sighting reports will be made available to
the public. You will wish to be aware that, as with other
government departments, MOD files are subject to the provisions of
the Public Records Act of 1958 and 1967. This Act of Parliament
states that official files generally remain closed from public
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken, and at
the thirty year point they are transferred to the Public Record
Office and into the public domain. It was generally the case that
before 1967 all "UFO" report files were destroyed after five years

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

because of limited archive space and due to insufficient public
interest in the subject, there was no reason for them to be
retained. Since 1967 due to an increase in public interest these
files are now routinely preserved. However, a few files from the
Fifties and Sixties did survive and may be viewed by members of
the public at the Public Record Office, Ruskin Avenue, Kew,
Richmond, Surrey, TW9 4DU. The references of these files are as
follows:

AIR 16/1199

AIR 20/7390 AIR 2/16918
AIR 20/9320 AIR 2/17318
AIR 20/9321 AIR 2/17526
AIR 20/9322 AIR 2/17527
AIR 20/9994 AIR 2/17982
PREM 11/855 AIR 2/17983

6. You will be interested to know that the Cabinet Office has
the responsibility for taking forward the Government's manifesto
pledge to introduce a Freedom of Information Act. The timetable
currently envisaged involves the publication of a White Paper
before the end of the year.

7. I hope this explains the position.

OPEN LETTER OF CENSURE TO THE BUFORA COUNCIL
To all BUFORA members

Originally having seen the Rudloe Manor presentation, I felt the need to write a letter which I was
originally going to call ‘COVER UP? OF COURSE THERE'S A COVER UP, BUT WHAT DOES IT
REALLY MATTER?. But then to be shown at the BUFORA Conference a video tape showing the Conference
organiser along wit nly trespassing on Ministry of Defence land ai RAF Bentwaters and
gaining access to what had appeared to be a previously secure hardened storage facility, made me feel
compelled to write this letter.

lam a BUFORA member. Both my husband and I investigate UFO sightings. We have willingly passed
the results of these investigations to BUFORA. We both have a lengthy and detailed knowledge of the subject.

First let me MM ection 40| that most military bases have underground facilities and yes, some
are indeed connected by subterranean transport systems. There is nothing unusual or sinister about this. It is
merely a military fact of life. Since the commencement of the Second World War, with increased and accurate
aerial and satellite surveillance, acrial bombardment and nuclear warfare, ii has become necessary for the
military to protect its supply and command and control structures in hardened underground facilities. If
Rudloe Manor was the Headquarters of RAF Southern Sector, I would find it surprising if it did not have
substantial underground facilities. Bui what has a detailed presentation of the military infrastructure to do
with UFOs?

Why BeesSection 40 Fra aggrieved with Ministry of Defence personnel, whom he states acted in a
hostile and aggressive manner towards him? Why does he feel the need to exact revenge? He made an
unauthorised incursion on to Ministry of Defence property.

My husband is a police officer in North Yorkshire. His beat area adjoins RAF Fylingdales. On his visits to
the base in an official capacity, he is accompanied by an armed military escort. He is not allowed to walk
freely around the base. Had such an unauthorised incursion onto military property not been met by a military
response, with or without drawn weapon, then those same military personnel would be guilty of neglect of
duty. There is a substantial terrorist threat in the United Kingdom and all military bases are on a permanent
state of heightened alert. Did to be treated differently? The military are tasked to defend
this country - that's me and you. I find nothing unusual in their use of covert and overt surveillance of persons
and vehicles loitering in the vicinity of sensitive military establishments. I think it is time to
exercise a degree of maturity. Incidentally I will be unable to look at a dog walker in green wellies and a wax
jacket in the same light again. You have destroyed my innocence!

But to return to a more serious note. The military's primary role is the defence of our country. The RAF in
particular are tasked to identify and evaluate, both radar and visual incursions into UK air space. I found it
perplexing that an hour of conference time should be given to proving that Air Intelligence investigated UFO
sightings in the 1950s and 60s. Of course they did. To think otherwise shows an amazing degree of naiveté.
From Roswell onwards any student of this subject will be aware that the military and the government have
covered things up, time and time again. There is nothing unusual in this There are.many valid reasons why
this should be so. They may not wish to display their ignorance of the an event and therefore display their
inability to counter a real or potential threat. There are factions in all societies who would exploit such
vulnerability and thus undermine the government and the military and in turn its ability to defend its peopie.
Perhaps they wish te-cever up-a crashed-experimental weaporr system. To do otherwise would be to expose its
level of weapon technology. Remember the Cold War has only recently ended. The former Soviet Union is

hardly a model of political stability. T is not a safe place peaceful _place- If only it were so-

My advice to RM Scction 40M to take a leaf out of Section 40, i He admits there
was a cover up at Rendlesham from day one. He does not become obsessed with that cover up but goes on to
probe the facts concealed behind it revealing truly amazing results. As UFO investigators we all acknowledge
that facts are covered up and official denials issued. We have come to expect this. Our task is to penetrate the
cover-up and reveal the concealed facts, --not to become obsessed with proving a cover up exists. It is easier to
investigate static terrestrial targets, rather than the elusive and enigmatic subject of unidentified flying objects.

Finally See have you considered the consequence of your actions? You seem inordinately proud
that you have over flown a sensitive military establishment and published photographs of it. Did you sign the
Official Secrets Act when you worked for Her Majesty's Customs and Excise? Your need to name
underground bases -underground command and control structures and the existence-of Intelligence Agencies at
Rudloe Manor, places this sensitive material in the public domain for the use of aggressors both internal and
external. Investigation without responsibility is dangerous.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

I believed when I joined BUFORA that it was a mature organisation dedicated to research into UFOs. To
my dismay, I find it members openly boasting their ability to make over flights of sensitive military
establishments, to make illegal and unexpected visits to RAF Rudloe Manor and to teespass.on to Ministry of
Defence Property at RAF Bentwaters and to gain entry to what appeared to be a previously locked facility. the
action of the trespassers on the video tape footage show that they knew what they were doing was wrong.

Contrary to} protestations, trespass, with or without acts of damage or thefi, is an offence against
civil law and an Association or its members found committing acts of pure trespass may. be sued in the Civil
Courts.

To find such a.-respected organisation as BUFORA actively or passively condoning such behaviour at its
Conference, is an act of suicidal folly. Recently the UFO movement has gained much credibility in the eyes of
the public and its past image of 'crankiness’ is beginning to disappear. But with its members committing such.
acts is to play directly into the hands of our detractors and opponents. It will give the authorities the excuse
they need to quietly withdraw any support or.co-operation that may have existed and sympathetic MPs may, in.
the future, go out of their way to avoid contact with such an organisation and its members. Ultimately it will
do the movement much harm.

I believe that UFOs are a topic worthy of serious; lengthy and detailed investigation. T feel that the study of
the subject will ultimately lead to a profound realignment in how we see ourselves, our place in the world and
the universe and perhaps our view of reality itself. It is a widely challenging and exciting topic. Fhe search.
for an answer will require hard and dedicated investigation, but it is primarily the study of unexplained aerial
phenomena. Both Rudioe Manor and RAF Bentwaters are identified and terrestrial.

I lay before the BUFORA Council a challenge to respond collectively to this letter. I know from the
comments of the Association's membership and the members of the public who attended the conference at
Hallam University that the Conference did much to harm the Associations public image.

Consider your reply carefully. Tam not sure that I wish to remain a member of an Association, which
either passively or actively condones such illegal and irresponsible acts. I fecl that the Association should
censure those individuals concerned and ask them te consider their future as members of BUFORA. The
Association must seem to operate at all times in an open responsible manner within the legal framework of this
country. Fhe Association and its membership are not above the law.

I feel that the emphasis on conspiracy theory will ultimately mean that BUFORA will lose its way in those
dark subterranean tunnels of the secret bases that seem to preoccupy so much time of the advocates of a sucha
theory. BUFORA is dedicated to research into UFOs, unexplained aerial phenomena. I feel the answer will
not be found underground.

With heartfelt concern,

Yours faithfully

1. Thank you for your letter of 25 August addressed to the
Secretary of State for Defence and copied to the Minister for the
Armed Forces. Your letter has been passed to this office which is
the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence
relating to the "UFO" phenomenon. I have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity. Unless there is evidence
of a potential military threat, and to date no "“UFO" report has
revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise
nature of each sighting reported to us.

3. Turning now to the main thrust of your letter, when the
Ministry of Defence was informed of the events which are alleged
to have occurred at Rendlesham Forest/RAF Woodbridge in December
1980, all available substantiated evidence was looked at in the
usual manner by those within the MOD/RAF with responsibility for
air defence matters. The judgement was that there was no
indication that a breach of the United Kingdom's air defences had
occurred on the nights in question. As there was no evidence to
substantiate an event of defence concern no further investigation
into the matter was necessary. Although a number of allegations
have subsequently been made about these reported events, nothing
has emerged over the last 16% years which has given us reason to
believe that the original assessment made by this Department was
incorrect.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.
