25 August 1997
Dear Secretary of State,

Was British Nuclear Base Under Hostile Threat?

Further details have recently emerged concerning a most extraordinary series of
incidents, which took place during the latter part of December 1980.

These events occurred at the twin-base complex of RAF Bentwaters and RAF
Woodbridge in Suffolk. American bases on British soil, they provided a strategically
important role in support of NATO's front-line defences.

On 13 January 1981, the Deputy Base Commander, Lieutenant-Colonel Charles Halt,
filed an official report to the Ministry of Defence and although the existence of any
such document was seemingly denied by the then British government, a copy was
obtained in the United States under their Freedom of Information Act.

The report, which came to be known as the Halt memo, is self explanatory and a copy
is attached for your information.

Subsequently promoted to Colonel and assigned to a highly sensitive security post,
Halt has since retired from the services. In a recent interview with award-winning
American journalist AJS Rayl, Colonel Halt expressed his bewilderment at the lack of
any response from the Ministry.

“To this day, I'm very puzzled why no-one ever came back and asked for additional
information, asked questions, or even interviewed me”, he notes.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Although questions relating to the Halt memo were subsequently raised in the House,
Lord Trefgarne, then Parliamentary Under-Secretary of State for the Armed Forces,
replied that, whilst he had seen the memorandum, the matter was not of concern from
a defence standpoint.

Now, important new witness testimony from former base personnel, no longer fearing
they may be breaching security, confirms that the incidents which were brought to the
Ministry's attention were of the utmost defence significance.

For Colonel Halt, who had maintained “there are many things which were not in my
memo”, has revealed that pencil-like beams of light, “like a laser beam” were emitted
by unidentified aerial craft and directed near the weapons storage area.

“It appeared to come toward us at a very high speed, stopped overhead and sent down
a small pencil-like beam, sort of like a laser beam”, he claims.

The object then moved back towards the RAF Bentwaters base and, according to Halt,
“continued to send down beams of light, at one point near the weapons storage
facility”.

He affirms, “We knew that, because we could hear the chatter on the radio” and
estimates that between 30 and 40 personnel stationed around the dual base complex
were witnesses.

Larry Warren, a former member of the 8ist Security Police Squadron stationed at the
bases, has also now published a book which claims that that the facility included “hot”
weapons with nuclear warheads and that the pencil-thin beams of light penetrated the
nuclear bunkers and compromised some of these weapons.

Halt's own disclosures are so alarming, that this further revelation seems no more
astonishing.

As referenced in Colonel Halt's memo to the Ministry, two nights previously, an
object suspected to have been a crashed aircraft, was spotted in the nearby
Rendlesham Forest, which separated the bases.

A central witness to that event was Staff Sergeant Jim Penniston, a member of the
8ist Security Police Squadron. Although Penniston has, similar to Colonel Halt,
previously given a summary account on his experiences, in a recent interview with
Rayl, Penniston for the first time discloses the full story of his encounter and its
aftermath.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Penniston states:

“T went to the gate shack, phoned in, and spoke with Master Sergeant J.D. Chandler,
who was the on-duty flight chief for both bases.

While I was talking with Chandler, the security controller was making contact with
Bentwaters Control Tower, and they, in turn, were in contact with Heathrow Airport's
Control Tower in London and RAF Bawdsey Eastern Radar in East Anglia. I was told
they reported a bogie about three miles out from Bentwaters but had lost contact with
it 15 minutes previously. The confirmation that triangulated the radar sighting was
RAF Bawdsey. It had been over Woodbridge, they told me, when it dropped from
radar imaging”.

Penniston and two other members of the security police, Airmen: First Class John
Burroughs and Ed Cabansag, obtained permission to proceed off base and investigate
the presumed aircrash.

However, what they discovered and reported was quite unexpected and is recorded in
Colonel Halt's report to the Ministry. Penniston now adds:

“T got to within 10 feet of the craft and the clearing where it sat. I estimated it to be
about three meters tall and about three meters wide at the base. No landing gear was
apparent, but it seemed like she was on fixed legs. I moved a little closer. I had
already taken all 36 pictures [of the object] on my roll of film. I walked around the
craft, and finally, I walked right up to the craft. I noticed the fabric of the shell was
more like a smooth, opaque, black glass. The bluish lights went from black to gray to
blue. I was pretty much confused at that point. I kept trying to put this in some kind of
frame of reference, trying to find some logical explanation as to what this was and
what was going on. It was dead silent. No animals were even making a noise
anymore.

On the smooth exterior shell there was writing of some kind, but I couldn't quite
distinguish it, so I moved up to it. It was three-inch lettering, rather symbols that
stretched for the length of two feet, maybe a little more. I touched the symbols, and I
could feel the shapes as if they were inscribed or etched or engraved, like a diamond
cut on glass.

At that point, I backed away from the craft, because the light was starting to get
brighter. Still, there was no sound. There was no physical contact with any kind of life
form, but there did seem to be a life presence. It was mechanical, this ship, and it
seemed to be under intelligent control.

The next thing I knew, I was standing about 20 feet away from the craft with
Burroughs, who I thought I had left back near the tree line. The craft moved up off the
ground, about three feet, still with absolutely no sound. It started to move slowly,
weaving back through the trees at a very slow pace, maybe a half a foot per second. It
took about a couple of minutes for it to manoeuvre itself back to a distance of about
100 to 150 feet, then it rose up just over the trees, about 200 feet high. There was a
momentary pause... and then literally with the blink of an eye it was gone. All with no
sound. That still boggles my mind”.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Penniston also now reveals that the following morning, he was debriefed for some 90
minutes by base personnel from the USAF's Office of Special Investigations (OSI)
and provided them with a full report.

He had examined the object at close range for some 20 minutes and taken notes,
which he has now released, however, he was later informed that the photographs
“hadn't turned out”.

Burroughs is also on record as confirming the reality of these events and noted, “You
felt like you were moving in slow motion, your hair on the back of your head was
standing up, you felt like you had very little control over your body”.

Adding further weight to that reality is a new witness who, learning of my interest in
the case, recently contacted me. He was part of the Security Police Squadron team
later sent to ensure the safety of Burroughs, Penniston and Cabansag.

Given the pseudonym of “Tom”, for the moment, and speaking of his involvement for
the first time in 17 years, he states:

“T worked with Burroughs and Penniston. What they said was true. As a matter of
fact, the following statements are true and can be verified by many people.

The acting squadron was the 81st Security Police Squadron and 'B' flight [duty shift]
was the acting security force on duty. Burroughs and Penniston were on 'B' flight and
the shift change was from 2300 hrs till 0700 hrs”.

“The person on the post should have been Burroughs, but he was missing, the
weapons were missing and the phone was off the hook. This was around 2:00 a.m. We
were carrying weapons and were told to hold our position. Master Sergeant Fail went

”

out”.

From this vantage point, Tom watched the lights in the forest. “I could clearly see the
lights from the gate, just outside the back gate, the East gate”, he states. He describes
the lights in the forest as being some distance from the ground, stationary and
pulsating.

Whatever the object was, according to Tom, “It made no noise, but it defied gravity. It
was really weird and scary”.

“I saw them, so did Airman Beachum, Airman Hartman, Airman Hernandez”, he
adds, mentioning some previously unknown witnesses. Some time later, “Burroughs
and Penniston finally showed up. We were then told to go back to our posts”.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Tom also described events and fresh devastation witnessed the following morning:

“Shift change in the morney for 'B' flight was extremely late. 'A' Flight relieved us at
9:30 am, very late. When we went out the East gate back to Bentwaters, leaving
Woodbridge, there were many people, including numerous people in typical lab coats
making an analysis of the area. You could see out the vehicle to the right side, that
there were many people examining the area, searching, looking for something. Some
of these people were in white overcoats, that's why I say that they looked like typical
‘techs' in lab coats. They were searching the area in front of the forest”.

“Tt wasn't just a few broken branches, a huge area had been cleared. There were trees
missing”. I also saw 'bobbies' and little white police cars”.

Whether that damage had perhaps been caused by an object coming down into the
forest, or manoeuvring through it, was unknown.

What is now known however, is that we have a number of corroborative testimonies,
originating from trained military personnel, that pose some fundamental questions.
There is of course much more to all of these testimonies than this brief summary
allows.

Now that key witnesses feel able to speak of these issues, does the Ministry agree they
should be asked to give testimonies before a committee and that these allegations
demand to be thoroughly investigated?

What was the unidentified “bogie”, corroborated on radar, which penetrated the
defence of one of NATO's most sensitive bases?

What was the enigmatic object, witnessed at length and documented by Sergeant Jim
Penniston and also witnessed first-hand by other base personnel?

What caused the substantial damage within Rendlesham Forest, close to the sensitive
facility?

During the second night's events, were lives endangered by the laser-like beams of
light directed near the weapons storage areas?

Was there danger of a catastrophic explosion?
Was this investigated at all by the Ministry of Defence, or did the Ministry simply

dismiss Colonel Halt' cursory report, unaware that the incidents were of such
magnitude?
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Colonel Halt is now on record as stating, “I intend someday to have my day in court
in England, publicly, if I have the opportunity”.

In the spirit of a more open government, I'm sure that the Ministry would welcome the

Colonel's desire for the truth to be determined and defence implications clarified.

I look forward to hearing from you on these matters and shall be happy to provide
copies of detailed testimonies from the aforementioned witnesses and others.

Before doing so, the Ministry may of course now be able to offer an explanation for
these reported occurrences and reassure the public why the incidents were found to be
of no defence significance.

Yours sincerely,

CC: The Rt Hon Dr John Reid MP
Minister of State for the Armed Forces

25 August 1997
Dear Secretary of State,
Was British Nuclear Base Under Hostile Threat?

Further details have recently emerged concerning a most extraordinary series of
incidents, which took place during the latter part of December 1980.

These events occurred at the twin-base complex of RAF. Bentwaters and RAF
Woodbridge in Suffolk. American bases on British soil, they provided a strategically
important role in support of NATO's front-line defences.

On 13 January 1981, the Deputy Base Commander, Lieutenant-Colonel Charles Halt,
filed an official report to the Ministry of Defence and although the existence of any
such document was seemingly denied by the then British government, a copy was
obtained in the United States under their Freedom of Information Act.

The report, which came to be known as the Halt memo, is self explanatory and a copy

is attached for your information.

Subsequently promoted to Colonel and assigned to a highly sensitive security post,
Halt has since retired from the services. In a recent interview with award-winning
American journalist AJS Rayl, Colonel Halt expressed his bewilderment at the lack of
any response from the Ministry.

“To this day, I'm very puzzled why no-one ever came back and asked for additional
information, asked questions, or even interviewed me”, he notes.

\

Telephone SIS a

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Although questions relating to the Halt memo were subsequently raised in the House,
Lord Trefgarne, then Parliamentary Under-Secretary of State for the Armed Forces,
replied that, whilst he had seen the memorandum, the matter was not of concern from
a defence standpoint.

Now, important new witness testimony from former base personnel, no longer fearing
they may be breaching security, confirms that the incidents which were brought to the
Ministry's attention were of the utmost defence significance.

For Colonel Halt, who had maintained “there are many things which were not in my
memo”, has revealed that pencil-like beams of light, “like a laser beam” were emitted
by unidentified aerial craft and directed near the weapons storage area.

“Tt appeared to come toward us at a very high speed, stopped overhead and sent down
a small pencil-like beam, sort of like a laser beam”, he claims.

The object then moved back towards the RAF Bentwaters base and, according to Halt,
“continued to send down beams of light, at one point near the weapons storage
facility”.

He affirms, “We knew that, because we could hear the chatter on the radio” and
estimates that between 30 and 40 personnel stationed around the dual base complex
were witnesses.

Larry Warren, a former member of the 81st Security Police Squadron stationed at the
bases, has also now published a book which claims that that the facility included “hot”
weapons with nuclear warheads and that the pencil-thin beams of light penetrated the
nuclear bunkers and compromised some of these weapons.

Halt's own disclosures are so alarming, that this further revelation seems no more
astonishing.

As referenced in Colonel Halt's memo to the Ministry, two nights previously, an
object suspected to have been a crashed aircraft, was spotted in the nearby
Rendlesham Forest, which separated the bases.

A central witness to that event was Staff Sergeant Jim Penniston, a member of the
81st Security Police Squadron. Although Penniston has, similar to Colonel Halt,
previously given a summary account on his experiences, in a recent interview with
Rayl, Penniston for the first time discloses the full story of his encounter and its
aftermath.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Penniston states:

“T went to the gate shack, phoned in, and spoke with Master Sergeant J.D. Chandler,
who was the on-duty flight chief for both bases.

While I was talking with Chandler, the security controller was making contact with
Bentwaters Control Tower, and they, in turn, were in contact with Heathrow Airport's
Control Tower in London and RAF Bawdsey Eastern Radar in East Anglia. I was told
they reported a bogie about three miles out from Bentwaters but had lost contact with
it 15 minutes previously. The confirmation that triangulated the radar sighting was
RAF Bawdsey. It had been over Woodbridge, they told me, when it dropped from
radar imaging”.

Penniston and two other members of the security police, Airmen First Class John
Burroughs and Ed Cabanissag, obtained permission to proceed off base and investigate
the presumed aircrash.

However, what they discovered and reported was quite unexpected and is recorded in
Colonel Halt's report to the Ministry. Penniston now adds:

“T got to within 10 feet of the craft and the clearing where it sat. I estimated it to be
about three meters tall and about three meters wide at the base. No landing gear was
apparent, but it seemed like she was on fixed legs. I moved a little closer. I had
already taken all 36 pictures [of the object] on my roll of film. I walked around the
craft, and finally, I walked right up to the craft. I noticed the fabric of the shell was
more like a smooth, opaque, black glass. The bluish lights went from black to gray to
blue. I was pretty much confused at that point. I kept trying to put this in some kind of
frame of reference, trying to find some logical explanation as to what this was and
what was going on. It was dead silent. No animals were even making a noise
anymore.

On the smooth exterior shell there was writing of some kind, but I couldn't quite
distinguish it, so I moved up to it. It was three-inch lettering, rather symbols that
stretched for the length of two feet, maybe a little more. I touched the symbols, and I
could feel the shapes as if they were inscribed or etched or engraved, like a diamond
cut on glass.

At that point, I backed away from the craft, because the light was starting to get
brighter. Still, there was no sound. There was no physical contact with any kind of life
form, but there did seem to be a life presence. It was mechanical, this ship, and it
seemed to be under intelligent control.

The next thing I knew, I was standing about 20 feet away from the craft with
Burroughs, who I thought I had left back near the tree line. The craft moved up off the
ground, about three feet, still with absolutely no sound. It started to move slowly,
weaving back through the trees at a very slow pace, maybe a half a foot per second. It
took about a couple of minutes for it to manoeuvre itself back to a distance of about
100 to 150 feet, then it rose up just over the trees, about 200 feet high. There was a
momentary pause... and then literally with the blink of an eye it was gone. All with no
sound. That still boggles my mind”.
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Penniston also now reveals that the following morning, he was debriefed for some 90
minutes by base personnel from the USAF's Office of Special Investigations (OSI)
and provided them with a full report.

He had examined the object at close range for some 20 minutes and taken notes,
which he has now released, however, he was later informed that the photographs
“hadn't turned out”.

Burroughs is also on record as confirming the reality of these events and noted, “You
felt like you were moving in slow motion, your hair on the back of your head was
standing up, you felt like you had very little control over your body”.

Adding further weight to that reality is a new witness who, learning of my interest in
the case, recently contacted me. He was part of the Security Police Squadron team
later sent to ensure the safety of Burroughs, Penniston and Cabansag.

Given the pseudonym of “Tom”, for the moment, and speaking of his involvement for
the first time in 17 years, he states:

“IT worked with Burroughs and Penniston. What they said was true. As a matter of
fact, the following statements are true and can be verified by many people.

The acting squadron was the 81st Security Police Squadron and 'B' flight [duty shift]
was the acting security force on duty. Burroughs and Penniston were on 'B' flight and
the shift change was from 2300 hrs till 0700 hrs”.

“The person on the post should have been Burroughs, but he was missing, the
weapons were missing and the phone was off the hook. This was around 2:00 a.m. We
were carrying weapons and were told to hold our position. Master Sergeant Fail went
out”,

From this vantage point, Tom watched the lights in the forest. “I could clearly see the
lights from the gate, just outside the back gate, the East gate”, he states. He describes
the lights in the forest as being some distance from the ground, stationary and
pulsating.

Whatever the object was, according to Tom, “It made no noise, but it defied gravity. It
was really weird and scary”.

“T saw them, so did Airman Beachum, Airman Hartman, Airman Hernandez”, he
adds, mentioning some previously unknown witnesses. Some time later, “Burroughs
and Penniston finally showed up. We were then told to go back to our posts”.

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Tom also described events and fresh devastation witnessed the following morning:

“Shift change in the morney for 'B' flight was extremely late. 'A' Flight relieved us at
9:30 am, very late. When we went out the East gate back to Bentwaters, leaving
Woodbridge, there were many people, including numerous people in typical lab coats
making an analysis of the area. You could see out the vehicle to the right side, that
there were many people examining the area, searching, looking for something. Some
of these people were in white overcoats, that's why I say that they looked like typical
‘techs’ in lab coats. They were searching the area in front of the forest”.

“Tt wasn't just a few broken branches, a huge area had been cleared. There were trees
missing”. I also saw ‘bobbies' and little white police cars”.

Whether that damage had perhaps been caused by an object coming down into the
forest, or manoeuvring through it, was unknown.

What is now known however, is that we have a number of corroborative testimonies,
originating from trained military personnel, that pose some fundamental questions.
There is of course much more to all of these testimonies than this brief summary
allows.

Now that key witnesses feel able to speak of these issues, does the Ministry agree they
should be asked to give testimonies before a committee and that these allegations
demand to be thoroughly investigated?

What was the unidentified “bogie”, corroborated on radar, which penetrated the
defence of one of NATO's most sensitive bases?

What was the enigmatic object, witnessed at length and documented by Sergeant Jim
Penniston and also witnessed first-hand by other base personnel?

What caused the substantial damage within Rendlesham Forest, close to the sensitive
facility?

During the second night's events, were lives endangered by the laser-like beams of
light directed near the weapons storage areas?

Was there danger of a catastrophic explosion?
Was this investigated at all by the Ministry of Defence, or did the Ministry simply

dismiss Colonel Halt! cursory report, unaware that the incidents were of such
magnitude?

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

Colonel Halt is now on record as stating, “I intend someday to have my day in court
in England, publicly, if I have the opportunity”.

In the spirit of a more open government, I'm sure that the Ministry would welcome the

Colonel's desire for the truth to be determined and defence implications clarified.

I look forward to hearing from you on these matters and shall be happy to provide
copies of detailed testimonies from the aforementioned witnesses and others.

Before doing so, the Ministry may of course now be able to offer an explanation for
these reported occurrences and reassure the public why the incidents were found to be
of no defence significance.

Yours sincerel:

CC: The Rt Hon Dr John Reid MP
Minister of State for the Armed Forces

1. Thank you for your recent letter addressed to the Prime
Minister regarding “unidentified flying objects". Your letter has
been passed to the Ministry of Defence and this office is the
focal point within the MOD for correspondence of this nature. I
have been asked to reply.

2 First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
Significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial Jlifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged

phenomena.

The attached letter(s) which the Prime Minister has received has been
forwarded to this Department for official action. No.10's letter codes are as
follows:

(a) - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
send a full reply within 20 working days.

B - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
consider whether there is anything which can usefully
be said to the correspondent and action accordingly.

Cc - No acknowledgement has been sent. In this case,
however, it is obviously important that both an
acknowledgement and a full reply are sent.

Unless specifically asked to do so, there is no need for you to copy your
replies to this office.

A new Open Government Code of Practice came into force in January 1997.
All replies to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
econ 40) 48/97; further information is available from DOMD on extension

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep record of
their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the
accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be performed
throughout the year. pemaet

Stansted Airport. As will now be aware, your letter has been
passed onto the Ministry of Defence for reply. This office is the
focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to
"unidentified flying objects".

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no “UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. I have looked back through our sighting report files and can

confirm that the MOD did not receive any reports from the
Peterborough/Wisbech area for 23 November 1996.

Milton Keynes, Date
Buckinghamshire. 3 September 1997
1. Thank you for your letter of 1 August 1997 in which you have

made enquiries about a sighting of an “unidentified flying object"
seen above Milton Keynes. This office is the focal. point within
the Ministry of Defence for correspondence of this nature and I
have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no “UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. With regard to the sighting over Milton Keynes on 28 July, we
are satisfied that there was no corroborating evidence to suggest
that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised
military aircraft. As stated above, that is the MOD's only

concern.

1. Thank you for your letter of 29 July in which you have asked
whether any military aircraft have been operating over the Derwent
Moors area. As this is in connection with a "UFO" query, your
letter has been passed to this office as we are the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
“unidentified flying objects".

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4, You asked whether military aircraft had been operating over
the Derwent Moors but as you did not specify a date I have not
been able to carry out a check. However, in principle, the whole
of the UK is open to low flying by military aircraft. There are
certain locations which are excluded such as major built-up areas
and restricted airspace around civil airfields, but outside such
exclusion zones less populated areas, such as the Derwent Moors,
will see a proportion of training flights from time to time.

Thank you for your letter of July 15th in reply to mine to you in which I made
an official complaint about the way your staff have been dodging questions
about UFOs which, if answered properly, would contradict the MoD's party line
on the matter.

I am beginning to find it sadly amusing that no one is prepared to answer
specifically how the MoD knows that the unexplained UFOs are not of defence
significance when you do not know what they are. It's almost as if your staff
and you are dyslexic and can not see these words as you continue to quote the
same old answer, or lack of it I should say. No doubt you ail see me as a big
pain writing all my antagonistic letters to Air Staff 2, but put yourself in
my shoes and think about your answers and compare them with the evidence for
UFOs from The PRO's files, the pilot's testimony (both civil and military) and
the numerous videos that have been taken. One of which was even corroborated
by Radar from RAF Lyneham. No wonder people such as myself are suspicious of
the MoD!

If it truly is ignorance of a very valid phenomenon then the MoD is being just
plain stupid, which I am sure they are not! For instance, the MoD has not
explained the video recorded sighting over Matlock on August 23rd 1994. Not
only is there a UFO on this video, there are helicopters too! Nothing to add
to calcio cura Being satisfied with that puts the UK's defence

1. Thank you for your letter of 27 June addressed to the Prime
Minister regarding "UFOs". Your letter has been passed to the
Ministry of Defence and this office is the focal point within the
MOD for correspondence of this nature. I have been asked to reply.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of “unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "“UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
"“UFO/flying saucer" matters or to the question of the existence or
otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which we remain
totally open-minded. I should add that to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
phenomena.

The attached letter(s) which the Prime Minister has received has been
forwarded to this Department for official action. No.10's letter codes are as
follows:

eS - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
send a full reply within 20 working days.

B - The letter has been acknowledged by No.10. Please
consider whether there is anything which can usefully
be said to the correspondent and action accordingly.

Cc - No acknowledgement has been sent. In this case,
however, it is obviously important that both an
acknowledgement and a full reply are sent.

Unless specifically asked to do so, there is no need for you to copy your
replies to this office.

A new Open Government Code of Practice came into force in January 1997.
All replies to members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set
out in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
ect 740] 48/97; further information is available from DOMD on extension

Under the Citizens' Charter Departments are now required to keep record of
their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to keep information on
the number of requests for information which refer to the Code of Practice
including details of the correspondent and the nature and date of the reply. In
addition, the Department is required to provide a record of the total number of
letters from members of the public and provide statistics (which may be based on a
valid sample) of its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

1. Thank you for your letter of 27 July on the subject of
“unidentified flying objects".

2. The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of "unidentified
flying objects" it receives solely to establish whether what was
seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether there
is any evidence that. the UK Air Defence Region might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Unless there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no “unidentified flying object" sighting has revealed such
evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
reported incident. We believe that down to earth explanations
could be found for these reports, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, if resources were diverted for this purpose but
it would be an inappropriate use of defence resources to provide
this kind of aerial identification service.

4, When the Ministry of Defence was informed of the events which
are alleged to have occurred at Rendlesham Forest/RAF Woodbridge
in December 1980, all available substantiated evidence was looked
at in the usual manner by those within the MOD/RAF with
responsibility for air defence matters. The judgement was that
there was no indication that a breach of the United Kingdom's air
defences had occurred on the nights in question.

5. As there was no evidence to substantiate an event of defence
concern no further investigation into the matter was necessary.
Although a number of allegations have subsequently been made about
these reported events, nothing has emerged over the last 16% years
which has given us reason to believe that the original assessment
made by this Department was incorrect.

Yours sincerely,

1. I am writing with reference to your message left on the
Secretariat (Air Staff) answerphone in which you reported an
"unidentified flying object" seen over Slapton Sands in Devon on
Monday 25 August. This office is the focal point within the
Ministry of Defence for correspondence of this nature.

2. First I should explain that the Ministry of Defence examines
any reports of "unidentified flying objects" it receives solely to
establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the UK
Air Defence Region might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorized foreign military activity.

3. Uniess there is evidence of a potential military threat, and
to date no "UFO" report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources
were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to
do so.

4. With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm
that we received no other reports of "UFO" sightings for 25 August
in or around Devon, and we are satisfied that there is no

corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

(Barcelona) D/Sec(AS) /64/3

1. The UK MOD's position with respect to “unidentified flying
objects" was set out in my letter of 7 March 1997, namely that we
examine any report received to determine whether there is a
defence interest. Unless there is evidence that the UK Air
Defence Region has been compromised by hostile foreign military
activity we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
reported sighting. We believe that down to earth explanations
could be found for these reports, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, if resources were diverted for this purpose but
it would be an inappropriate use of defence resources to provide
this kind of aerial identification service.

2. With regard to access to the UK MOD's "UFO" report files you
may be interest to know that as is the case with other UK
government departments, MOD files are subject to the provisions of
the Public Records Act of 1958 and 1967. This Act of Parliament
states that official files generally remain closed from public
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. Those
files selected for preservation are then transferred to the Public
Record Office for release into the public domain.

3. It was generally the case that before 1967 all "UFO" files
were destroyed after five years as there was insufficient public
interest in the subject to merit their permanent retention.
However, since 1967 there has been an increase in public interest
in this subject and "UFO" report files are now routinely
preserved. A few files from the 1950s and early 1960s did,
however, survive and are available for examination by members of
the public. They may be viewed at the Public Record Office,
Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey, TW9 4DU. The references of
these files are as follows:

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/1995/1

AIR 20/9320 AIR 2/17526
AIR 20/9321 AIR 2/17527
AIR 20/9322 AIR 2/17982
AIR 20/9994 AIR 2/17983
PREM 11/855

All surviving paperwork from over 30 years ago on the subject of
"UFOs" previously held by the MOD has now been transferred to the

Public Record Office.

Yours sincerely,
